) arising from this initial data. Let
T_{\mathrm{max}}\in(0,\infty]
be the maximal time such that the solution
f
to the Vlasov–Poisson system is defined on
[0,T_{\mathrm{max}})
. By continuity, there exists a largest time
T\in(0,T_{\mathrm{max}}]
and a constant
C_{\mathrm{boot}}>0
such that the following bootstrap assumptions hold:

(BA1)

For all
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
and any
|\beta|\leq N-1
, we have

\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}Z^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\Big|\leq\dfrac{C_{\mathrm{boot}}\epsilon}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}.

(BA2)

For all
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
and any
|\beta|=N
, we have

\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}Z^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\Big|\leq\frac{C_{\mathrm{boot}}\epsilon\,\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}.

We will improve these estimates when
\epsilon>0
is small enough, for
C_{\mathrm{boot}}>0
chosen large enough.

Structure of the proof of small data global existence

(a)

As a first step, we prove decay estimates in time for the force field
\nabla_{x}\phi
and its derivatives
\nabla_{x}Z^{\gamma}\phi
.

(b)

Then, we consider a suitable modification of the linear weight
\langle x-vt\rangle
denoted by
{\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}}
, which is preserved by the non-linear Vlasov equation. We prove that the correction
{\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}}~-~\langle x-vt\rangle
grows logarithmically in time.

(c)

We prove that for every
|\beta|\leq N
, a weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
norm of
Z^{\beta}f
grows at most as
t^{\frac{1}{4}}
. This will suffice to improve the bootstrap assumption
(BA2)
.

(d)

Finally, we control uniformly in time the weighted spatial averages of
Z^{\beta}f
with
|\beta|\leq N-1
. These estimates will allow us to obtain optimal time decay for the velocity averages
\rho(Z^{\beta}f)
for every
|\beta|\leq N-1
. In particular, we will improve the bootstrap assumption
(BA1)
.

5.2.
Pointwise decay estimates for the force field

We start by controlling an integral term related to the Green function of the Laplacian on
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
.

Lemma 5.2.1
.

There holds

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{y}^{3}}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y|^{2}(1+|x+y|)^{3}}\leq 7\pi.

Proof.

We first remark that

\int_{|y|\leq 1}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y|^{2}(1+|x+y|)^{3}}\leq\int_{r=0}^{1}\int_{\theta=0}^{\pi}\int_{\varphi=0}^{2\pi}\frac{r^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r}{r^{2}}{\mathrm{d}}\theta{\mathrm{d}}\varphi\leq 4\pi.

We deal with the remaining region by applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\int_{|y|\geq 1}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y|^{2}(1+|x+y|)^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\int_{|y|\geq 1}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y|^{4}}\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\bigg|\int_{|y|\geq 1}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{(1+|x+y|)^{6}}\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq 2\sqrt{\pi}\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{(1+|z|)^{6}}\bigg|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{4\pi}{\sqrt{3}}.

∎

By Lemma
5.2.1
, we obtain decay in time for the integral term

(15)

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y|^{2}(1+t+|x-y|)^{3}}=\dfrac{1}{t^{2}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}z}{|z|^{2}(t^{-1}+1+|z-\frac{x}{t}|)^{3}}\leq\dfrac{7\pi}{t^{2}},

by using the change of variables
y=tz
. We use the estimate (
15
) to obtain decay for
\nabla_{x}Z^{\gamma}\phi
.

Proposition 5.2.2
.

For any
|\kappa|\leq N-1
and all
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
, we have

t^{|\kappa|}\big|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi\big|(t,x)\lesssim\dfrac{\epsilon}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}.

For the top order derivatives
|\kappa|=N
, there holds

t^{|\kappa|}\big|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi\big|(t,x)\lesssim\dfrac{\epsilon}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{7}{4}}}.

Proof.

Let
(t,x)\in[0,T_{\mathrm{max}})\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
. Using
[\Delta,\partial_{x^{i}}]=0
and the vector fields
G_{i}
, we obtain

\Delta_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi=\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\rho(f)=\rho\big(\partial_{x}^{\kappa}f\big)=t^{-|\kappa|}\rho\big(G^{\kappa}f\big),

for every
|\kappa|\leq N
. We use the Green function for the Poisson equation on
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
to write the gradient of the solution to the commuted Poisson equation as

(16)

\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi(t,x)=\frac{1}{4\pi t^{|\kappa|}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{y}^{3}}\dfrac{y}{|y|^{3}}\rho(G^{\kappa}f)(t,x-y)\mathrm{d}y,

for every
|\kappa|\leq N
. Thus, for any
|\kappa|\leq N
we obtain

\displaystyle t^{|\kappa|}|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi(t,x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{y}^{3}}\dfrac{\mathrm{d}y}{|y|^{3}\langle t+|x-y|\rangle^{3}}\big\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}\rho\big(G^{\kappa}f\big)(t,x)\big\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\big\|\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}\rho\big(G^{\kappa}f\big)(t,x)\big\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x})},

by using (
15
). The estimate on the lower order derivatives
|\kappa|\leq N-1
follows from the bootstrap assumption
(BA1)
, whereas the one for the top order derivatives
|\kappa|=N
ensue from
(BA2)
.
∎

5.3.
The modified weights

Since we expect
x-tv
to grow as
\log(t)
along the nonlinear flow, we will work with the following modification of this linear weight.

Definition 5.3.1
.

Let
\varphi=(\varphi^{1},\varphi^{2},\varphi^{3}):[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
be the unique solution to

\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(\varphi^{i})=-\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(x^{i}-tv^{i}),\qquad\varphi(0,x,v)=0.

We define the
modified weight function

\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}
as

\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}(t,x,v):=\big\langle x-tv+\varphi(t,x,v)\big\rangle.

One important property of the weight
\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}
is that it is, by definition, constant along the nonlinear flow. In order to exploit this property, we need to show that
{\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}}
does not deviate too much from the linear weight
x-tv
.

Lemma 5.3.2
.

There holds
{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}})=0
. Moreover, the correction
\varphi
satisfies that

\forall\,(t,x,v)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},\qquad|\varphi|(t,x,v)\lesssim\epsilon\log\langle t\rangle,\qquad|\nabla_{v}\varphi|(t,x,v)\lesssim\epsilon\,\langle t\rangle.

Proof.

The first property is straightforward, since we have defined
\varphi
so that
{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}})=0
. Next, we have

\left|\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(x^{i}-tv^{i})\right|=\big|\nabla_{x}\phi(t,x)\cdot\nabla_{v}(x^{i}-tv^{i})|\lesssim t|\nabla_{x}\phi|(t,x)\lesssim\epsilon\,\langle t\rangle^{-1},

for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. We then get
|\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(\varphi)|\lesssim\epsilon\,\langle t\rangle^{-1}
on
[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, which implies the estimate for
\varphi
according to Duhamel formula.

To conclude the proof it suffices to show, as
\nabla_{v}=G-t\nabla_{x}
, that there exists
C>0
such that

(17)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{x}\varphi|(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon,

(18)

\displaystyle|G\varphi|(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon\log\langle t\rangle,

for all
(t,x,v)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
. By continuity, there exists a maximal time
0<T_{\mathrm{boot}}\leq T
such that (
17
)–(
18
) holds on
[0,T_{\mathrm{boot}})\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
. Let us prove by a bootstrap argument that
T_{\mathrm{boot}}=T
. Consider
Z\in\boldsymbol{\lambda}
and apply the commutation formula in Proposition
2.5.2
to show

\displaystyle{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(Z\varphi)

\displaystyle=[{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi},Z](\varphi)+Z{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(\varphi)=-\nabla_{x}Z_{x}\phi\cdot\nabla_{v}\varphi+t\nabla_{x}Z_{x}\phi+t[Z_{x},\nabla_{x}]\phi.

Writing again
\nabla_{v}=G-t\nabla_{x}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(Z\varphi)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim t\big|\nabla_{x}Z_{x}\phi\big|\big(1+\big|\nabla_{x}\varphi\big|\big)+\big|\nabla_{x}Z_{x}\phi\big|\big|G\varphi\big|,

\displaystyle\big|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(\partial_{x^{i}}\varphi)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim t\big|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x^{i}}\phi\big|\big(1+\big|\nabla_{x}\varphi\big|+\big|G\varphi\big|\big).

We then deduce from the pointwise decay estimates of Proposition
5.2.2
as well as from the bootstrap assumptions (
17
)–(
18
) for the derivatives of
\varphi
that, for all
(t,x,v)\in[0,T_{\mathrm{boot}})\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
we have

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(Z\varphi)\right|(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-1}\left(1+C\epsilon+C\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-1}\log\langle t\rangle\right)\lesssim\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-1}(1+C\epsilon),

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(\partial_{x^{i}}\varphi)\right|(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-2}\left(1+\epsilon\log\langle t\rangle\right)\lesssim\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}(1+C\epsilon).

Hence, if
C
is chosen large enough and if
\epsilon
is small enough, we have

\left|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(Z\varphi)\right|(t,x,v)\leq\frac{C}{2}\epsilon\,\langle t\rangle^{-1},\qquad\left|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(\partial_{x^{i}}\varphi)\right|(t,x,v)\leq\frac{C}{2}\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

Using that
\varphi
initially vanishes and Duhamel’s formula, we improve (
17
)–(
18
) on
[0,T_{\mathrm{boot}})
. As a result, we have
T_{\mathrm{boot}}=T
as well as the stated estimate for
\nabla_{v}\varphi
.
∎

We then introduce the
modified velocity weight function
.

Definition 5.3.3
.

Let
w=(w^{1},w^{2},w^{3})\colon[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
be the unique solution to

\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(w^{i})=-\mathbf{T}_{\phi}(v^{i}),\qquad w(0,x,v)=0.

We define the
modified velocity weight function

\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{mod}}(t,x,v):=\langle v+w(t,x,v)\rangle
.

The next result implies that
v
is almost conserved by the nonlinear flow, that is
|v-\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{mod}}|\lesssim\epsilon
.

Lemma 5.3.4
.

We have
{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{mod}})=0
and
|w|(t,x,v)\lesssim\epsilon
for all
(t,x,v)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
.

Proof.

The first relation ensues from
{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(v+w)=0
. For the second one, we remark that for
1\leq i\leq 3
we have

(19)

\left|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(w^{i})\right|=\left|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(v^{i})\right|=|\partial_{x^{i}}\phi|(t,x)\lesssim\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-2}

according to the decay estimate for the force field in Proposition
5.2.2
. The result then follows from Duhamel formula as well as
w(0,\cdot,\cdot)=0
.
∎

5.4.
Pointwise estimates for the distribution function and its derivatives

We are now able to prove upper bounds for the weighted derivatives
\langle v\rangle^{M_{v}}\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{M_{z}}Z^{\beta}f
. Recall the initial norm
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{x},N_{v}}[f_{0}]
introduced in (
12
) and that
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{8,7}[f_{0}]\leq\epsilon
. For simplicity, we will consider
N\geq 2
. We sketch the proof of the case
N=1
in Remark
5.4.2
.

Proposition 5.4.1
.

Let
N_{x}\geq 0
,
N_{v}\geq 0
,
N\geq 2
, and assume that
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{x},N_{v}}[f_{0}]<\infty
. Then, there exists a constant
\varepsilon_{N}>0
depending only on
N
, such that the following statement holds. If
\epsilon\leq\varepsilon_{N}
, then, for all
(t,x,v)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

(20)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\square\square\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{v}}\,\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{x}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}G^{\beta_{v}}f\big|(t,x,v)\leq 3\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{x},N_{v}}[f_{0}]\log^{|\beta_{v}|}(2+t),\qquad

\displaystyle\textrm{ if }|\beta_{x}|+|\beta_{v}|\leq N-1,

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\square\square\qquad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{v}}\,\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{x}}\big|Z^{\beta}f\big|(t,x,v)\leq 3\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{x},N_{v}}[f_{0}]\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}},\qquad

\displaystyle\textrm{ if }|\beta|=N.

Remark 5.4.1
.

By Lemma
5.3.4
and the inequality
(a+b)^{z}\leq 2^{z-1}(a^{z}+b^{z})
, there exists
c>0
such that
\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\leq 2^{\frac{N_{v}}{2}-1}\,\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{v}}+2^{\frac{N_{v}}{2}-1}|c\epsilon|^{N_{v}}
.

Proof.

By continuity, there exists
0<T_{0}\leq T
such that (
20
) holds on
[0,T_{0})
, and let us prove that we can improve these two estimates if
\epsilon
is small enough, which would imply that they in fact hold on
[0,T)
. The starting point of the analysis consists in writing, for a fixed
|\beta|\leq N
,

(21)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\left(\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{v}}\,\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{x}}Z^{\beta}f\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{v}}\,\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{x}}{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(Z^{\beta}f),

where we used
{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{mod}})={\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}})=0
. By the commutation formula in Lemma
2.5.2
and
\nabla_{v}=G-t\nabla_{x}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\big(Z^{\beta}f\big)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{|\alpha|\leq|\beta|-1,}\,\sup_{\gamma=\beta-\alpha}|\nabla_{x}Z^{\gamma}_{x}\phi\cdot\nabla_{v}Z^{\alpha}f|

(22)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{|\alpha|\leq|\beta|-1,}\,\sup_{\gamma=\beta-\alpha}t|\nabla_{x}Z^{\gamma}_{x}\phi||\nabla_{x}Z^{\alpha}f|+|\nabla_{x}Z^{\gamma}_{x}\phi||GZ^{\alpha}f|.

Fix
\gamma
and
\alpha
such that
|\alpha|\leq|\beta|-1
and
\gamma+\alpha=\beta
. Then, we have

Z^{\beta}=\partial_{x}^{\beta_{x}}G^{\beta_{v}},\qquad Z^{\gamma}_{x}=t^{|\gamma_{v}|}\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{x}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{v}},\qquad Z^{\alpha}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}G^{\alpha_{v}},

where
\beta_{x}=\gamma_{x}+\alpha_{x}
, and
\beta_{v}=\gamma_{v}+\alpha_{v}
. We deal first with the case
|\beta|\leq N-1
. The goal consists in identyfing a hierarchy, indexed by
\beta_{v}
, in the different derivatives
Z^{\beta}f
. We start by controlling the easiest term in (
22
). According to Proposition
5.2.2
, we have

(23)

\big|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\big(Z^{\beta}f\big)\big|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\xi_{x}|+|\xi_{v}|\leq|\beta|\\
|\xi_{v}|\leq|\beta_{v}|+1\end{subarray}}|\partial_{x}^{\xi_{x}}G^{\xi_{v}}f|+\sup_{|\alpha|\leq|\beta|-1,}\,\sup_{\gamma=\beta-\alpha}t|\nabla_{x}Z^{\gamma}_{x}\phi||\nabla_{x}Z^{\alpha}f|.

For the first terms on the RHS, even if the number of Galilean vector fields can be larger than
|\beta_{v}|
, the decay rate
t^{-2}
is strong enough in order to handle them. For the most problematic terms, we will crucially exploit the condition
\gamma+\alpha=\beta
. Then,

•

either
|\gamma_{x}|\geq 1
, in which case
|\nabla_{x}Z^{\gamma}_{x}\phi|(t,x)\lesssim\epsilon\,\langle t\rangle^{-3}
by Proposition
5.2.2
,

•

or
|\alpha_{v}|\leq|\beta_{v}|-1
. Indeed, as
\gamma+\alpha=\beta
and
|\alpha|\leq|\beta|-1
, we have
|\gamma|\geq 1
. So
|\gamma_{x}|=0
implies that
|\gamma_{v}|\geq 1
and it remains to use
\gamma_{v}+\alpha_{v}=\beta_{v}
.

Consequently, there exists a constant
C(N)>0
, depending only on
N
, such that

\big|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\big(Z^{\beta}f\big)\big|\leq\frac{\epsilon C(N)}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\xi_{x}|+|\xi_{v}|\leq|\beta|\\
|\xi_{v}|\leq|\beta_{v}|+1\end{subarray}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\xi_{x}}G^{\xi_{v}}f\big|+\frac{\epsilon C(N)}{\langle t\rangle}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\alpha_{x}|+|\alpha_{v}|\leq|\beta|\\
|\alpha_{v}|\leq|\beta_{v}|-1\end{subarray}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}G^{\alpha_{v}}f\big|.

We then deduce from (
21
) and the bootstrap assumption (
20
) that there exists
\overline{C}(N)>0
such that, for all
(t,x,v)\in[0,T_{0})\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
we have

\Big|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\left(\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{v}}\,\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{x}}Z^{\beta}f\right)\Big|(t,x,v)\leq\epsilon\overline{C}(N)\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{x},N_{v}}[f_{0}]\bigg(\frac{\log^{|\beta_{v}|+1}(2+t)}{\langle t\rangle^{2}}+\frac{\log^{|\beta_{v}|-1}(2+t)}{\langle t\rangle}\bigg).

We then deduce from Duhamel’s principle that, for all
(t,x,v)\in[0,T_{0})\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}

\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{v}}\,\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{x}}\big|Z^{\beta}f\big|(t,x,v)\leq\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{x},N_{v}}[f_{0}]+\epsilon\widetilde{C}(N)\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{x},N_{v}}[f_{0}]\log^{|\beta_{v}|}(2+t),

where
\widetilde{C}(N)>0
depends only on
N
. If
\epsilon
is small enough, this improves the bootstrap assumption (
20
).

We now focus on the top order derivatives
|\beta|=N
. The difference here is that the case
|\gamma|=N
is allowed and that the top order derivatives of the force field decay at a slightly slower decay rate,

|\nabla_{x}Z^{\gamma}_{x}\phi|(t,x)=t^{|\gamma_{v}|}|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{x}}\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{v}}\phi|(t,x)\lesssim t^{-\frac{7}{4}-|\gamma_{x}|}.

Note however that
|\gamma|=N
implies
|\alpha|=0
in (
22
), and
\nabla_{x}f
is uniformly bounded. We then get according to (
20
) and Proposition
5.2.2
that

\displaystyle\sup_{|\beta|=N}\big|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}\big(\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{v}}\,\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{x}}Z^{\beta}f\big)\big|(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\log^{N-1}(2+t)}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{7}{4}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{x},N_{v}}[f_{0}]+\frac{\epsilon}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{3}{4}}}\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{x},N_{v}}[f_{0}]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\epsilon}{\langle t\rangle}\sup_{|\kappa|=N}\mathbf{v}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{v}}\,\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{N_{x}}|Z^{\kappa}f|(t,x,v).

The estimate for the top order derivatives then ensues from Duhamel’s principle and the Gronwall lemma for
\epsilon
small enough.
∎

Remark 5.4.2
.

The case
N=1
could be handled by slightly modifying the previous proof. We would have to prove first that
|\nabla_{x}f|
remains uniformly bounded using
|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(\nabla_{x}f)|(t,x,v)\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3/2}
. Then,
Gf
can be controlled as we bounded
Z^{\beta}f
for
|\beta|=N
.

Recall that
g_{0}(t,x,v):=f(t,x+tv,v)
, so that
\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g_{0}(t,x,v)=[\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}G^{\kappa_{v}}f](t,x+tv,v)
. We then obtain the next result by applying Proposition
5.4.1
as well as Lemma
5.3.2
. We also use
\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}(t,x+tv,v)-\langle x\rangle\leq|\varphi|(t,x,v)\lesssim\epsilon\log\langle t\rangle
.

Corollary 5.4.2
.

For any
|\kappa_{x}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N
and all
(t,x,v)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\langle x\rangle^{8}\langle v\rangle^{7}\big|\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g_{0}\big|(t,x,v)\lesssim\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\epsilon\log^{|\kappa_{v}|+8}(2+t)&\mbox{if $|\kappa_{x}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N-1$, }\\
\epsilon\,\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}\log^{8}(2+t)&\mbox{if $|\kappa_{x}|+|\kappa_{v}|=N$}.\end{array}\right..

5.5.
Uniform boundedness of spatial averages

We proceed to show uniform boundedness in time of the spatial averages

(24)

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}G^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x.

Remark 5.5.1
.

We restrict the analysis to the derivatives
G^{\beta}
since the spatial average of
\partial_{x}^{\kappa}G^{\beta}f
vanishes as soon as
|\kappa|\geq 1
. This follows from integration by parts, the spatial decay properties of
f
, and the bounds for its derivatives given in Proposition
5.4.1
.

Proposition 5.5.1
.

Let
|\beta|\leq N-1
. Then, for all
(t,v)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\bigg|\partial_{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}Z^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{7}{4}}}\sup_{|\kappa|\leq|\beta|+1}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\big|\mathbf{z}^{4}_{\mathrm{mod}}Z^{\kappa}f(t,x,v)\big|.

If
|\beta|\leq N-2
, then the factor
\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{7}{4}}
can be upgraded to
\langle t\rangle^{-2}
.

Proof.

Fix
(t,v)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
and
|\beta|\leq N-1
. Integrating the commutation formula of Proposition
2.5.2
for
Z^{\beta}f
and performing integration by parts in
x
, we have,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}Z^{\beta}f\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\big(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}\big)Z^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\mu\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\nabla_{x}\phi\cdot\nabla_{v}Z^{\beta}f{\mathrm{d}}x+\mu\sum_{|\alpha|\leq|\beta|-1}\,\sum_{\gamma=\beta-\alpha}C^{\beta}_{\alpha\gamma}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\nabla_{x}Z^{\gamma}_{x}\phi\cdot\nabla_{v}Z^{\alpha}f{\mathrm{d}}x.

Using
\nabla_{v}=G-t\nabla_{x}
and performing again integration by parts in
x
, we get

\bigg|\partial_{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}Z^{\beta}f\mathrm{d}x\bigg|\lesssim\sum_{|\gamma|+|\kappa|\leq|\beta|}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\big|\nabla_{x}Z^{\gamma}_{x}\phi\big|\big|GZ^{\kappa}f\big|\mathrm{d}x+t\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\big|\nabla_{x}\nabla_{x}Z^{\gamma}_{x}\phi\big|\big|Z^{\kappa}f\big|\mathrm{d}x.

We next use the time decay of the force field in Proposition
5.2.2
to obtain that for all
(t,v)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\bigg|\partial_{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}Z^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\bigg|(t,v)\lesssim\frac{\epsilon}{\langle t\rangle^{\frac{7}{4}}}\sup_{|\kappa|\leq|\beta|+1,}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\big|\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{4}Z^{\kappa}f(t,x,v)\big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}y}{\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{4}(t,y,v)}.

It remains us to prove that

(25)

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}y}{\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{4}(t,y,v)}\lesssim\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}z}{\langle z\rangle^{4}}\leq 4.

For this, we recall the estimate
|\nabla_{x}\varphi|\lesssim\epsilon
, that allows us to perform the change of variables
z=y-tv+\varphi(t,y,v)
for
\epsilon
small enough. The better estimate in the case
|\beta|\leq N-2
is related to the better decay properties verified by the lower order derivatives of the force field
\nabla_{x}Z^{\xi}\phi
for
|\xi|\leq N-1
.
∎

Combining Proposition
5.5.1
with the pointwise estimates in Proposition
5.4.1
, we obtain the following.

Corollary 5.5.2
.

For all
(t,v)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{v}^{3}
and every
|\beta|\leq N-1
, we have

\langle v\rangle^{3}\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}Z^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\bigg|\lesssim\epsilon.

5.6.
Pointwise decay estimates for velocity averages

In this subsection, we show that the decay rate of
\rho(Z^{\beta}f)
for
|\beta|\leq N-1
, coincides with the one of the linearised system. In particular, we improve the bootstrap assumption
(BA1)
. The starting point of the analysis consists in performing the change of variables
y=x-tv
.

Proposition 5.6.1
.

For any
|\beta|\leq N-1
, we have that for all
(t,x)\in[2,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
,

\bigg|t^{3}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}Z^{\beta}f(t,x,v){\mathrm{d}}v-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}Z^{\beta}f\Big(t,y,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y\bigg|\lesssim\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\epsilon\,\langle x/t\rangle^{-3}\,t^{-1}\log^{N}(t)&\mbox{if $|\beta|\leq N-2$, }\\[2.0pt]
\epsilon\,\langle x/t\rangle^{-3}\,t^{-2/3}&\mbox{if $|\beta|=N-1$}.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Let
|\beta|\leq N-1
and recall that
\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g_{0}(t,x,v)=Z^{\beta}f(t,x+tv,v)
. Fix
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
and perform the change of variables
y=x-tv
to get

\displaystyle t^{3}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}Z^{\beta}f(t,x,v){\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}\big[\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g_{0}\big](t,x-tv,v){\mathrm{d}}v=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}\big[\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g_{0}\big]\Big(t,y,\frac{x-y}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}\big[\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g_{0}\big]\Big(t,y,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y+\int_{\tau=0}^{1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}\frac{y}{t}\cdot\big[\nabla_{v}\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g_{0}\big]\Big(t,y,\frac{x-\tau y}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\tau.

We remark that the spatial average of
\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g_{0}
is equal to the one of
Z^{\beta}f
. It only remains to deal with the last term on the RHS of the previous equality. For this, note that

\forall\,\tau\in[0,1],\qquad 3\Big\langle\frac{x-\tau y}{t}\Big\rangle+\big\langle y+\varphi(t,y+tv,v)\big\rangle=\Big\langle\frac{x-\tau y}{t}\Big\rangle+\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}(t,y+tv,v)\geq\Big\langle\frac{x}{t}\Big\rangle.

Indeed, either
|y+\varphi(t,y+tv,v)|\geq\frac{1}{2}|x|
, in which case there is nothing to prove. Otherwise, it suffices to use that

2|x-\tau y|-|x|\geq-2\tau|\varphi(t,y+tv,v)|\gtrsim-\epsilon\log(1+t)\gtrsim-\epsilon t.

As
|y|\leq\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}(t,y+tv,v)+\epsilon\log(t)
and
y\mapsto\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{-4}(t,y+tv,v)\in L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y})
by (
25
), we then deduce

\bigg|\int_{\tau=0}^{1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}\frac{y}{t}\cdot\nabla_{v}\partial_{x,v}^{\beta}g_{0}\Big(t,y,\frac{x-\tau y}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}\tau\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\log(t)}{t\langle x/t\rangle^{3}}\!\sup_{(z,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\!\langle v\rangle^{3}\big|\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{8}GZ^{\beta}f\big|(t,z,v).

We finally bound the RHS by applying Proposition
5.4.1
for
(N_{x},N_{v})=(8,3)
.
∎

Proposition
5.6.1
allows us to deduce the following pointwise decay estimates.

Corollary 5.6.2
.

For any
|\beta|\leq N-1
, we have

\forall\,(t,x)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x},\qquad\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}Z^{\beta}f(t,x,v){\mathrm{d}}v\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}.

Proof.

Let
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
and remark that if
t\leq 2
, one only has to use

\langle x\rangle^{3}\,\langle v\rangle^{4}\big|Z^{\beta}f\big|(t,x,v)\lesssim\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{3}(t,x,v)\,\langle v\rangle^{4}\big|Z^{\beta}f\big|(t,x,v)\lesssim\epsilon.

Assume now that
t\geq 2
. According to Corollary
5.5.2
, we have

\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}Z^{\beta}f\Big(t,x,\frac{x}{t}\Big)\mathrm{d}x\bigg|\lesssim\epsilon\langle x/t\rangle^{-3}.

The result ensues from Proposition
5.6.1
and
2t\langle x/t\rangle\geq t+|x|
.
∎

We now improve the bootstrap assumption
(BA2)
. For this, we will use the following lemma.

Lemma 5.6.3
.

Let
h:[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a regular function. Then, for all
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
, we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}|h|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}\sup_{(y,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}\langle v\rangle^{7}\big|\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{7}\,h\big|(t,y,v).

Proof.

The case
t\leq 1
and
|x|\leq 1
is straightforward since the map
v\mapsto\langle v\rangle^{-4}
belongs to
L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
. Let
t\geq 1
. We observe that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}|h(t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}v\leq\sup_{(y,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}\big|\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{4}\,h\big|(t,y,v)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}\frac{\mathrm{d}v}{\langle x-tv+\varphi(t,x,v)\rangle^{4}}.

The change of variables
y=x-tv
yields

t^{3}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}|h(t,x,v)|\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\sup_{(y,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}\big|\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{4}\,h\big|(t,y,v)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle y+\varphi(t,x,(x-y)t^{-1})\rangle^{4}}.

Let
\phi:y\mapsto y+\varphi(t,x,(x-y)t^{-1})
and
\psi=\mathrm{Id}-\phi
. In order to perform the change of variables
w=\phi(y)
, we will prove that
\frac{1}{2}\leq|\det(\mathrm{d}\phi(y))|\leq 2
for all
y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, provided
\epsilon
is small enough. This property is satisfied since

\forall\,(t,x,y)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y},\qquad|\mathrm{d}\psi(y)|\leq t^{-1}|\nabla_{v}\varphi|\big(t,x,(x-y)t^{-1}\big)\lesssim\epsilon,

according to Lemma
5.3.2
. We then deduce

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{\langle y+\varphi(t,x,(x-y)t^{-1})\rangle^{4}}\leq 2\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{w}}\frac{\mathrm{d}w}{\langle w\rangle^{4}}\leq 4.

We have then proved that

(26)

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}|h|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3}\sup_{(y,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}\big|\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{4}\,h\big|(t,y,v)+\langle v\rangle^{4}|h|(t,y,v),

for all
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
. To obtain the spatial decay and conclude the proof, we remark that

|x|\leq|x-tv|+t|v|\leq 2\log(3+t)\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}(t,x,v)+t\langle v\rangle\leq 2\langle t\rangle\big(\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}(t,x,v)+\langle v\rangle\big)

in view of Lemma
5.3.2
. We finally apply (
26
) to
\langle v\rangle^{3}h
as well as to
\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}^{3}h
.
∎

Remark 5.6.1
.

Note that a simpler proof provides

\forall\,(t,x)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x},\qquad\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}|h|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}\sup_{(y,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}\langle y-tv\rangle^{7}\langle v\rangle^{7}|h|(t,y,v).

Applying Lemma
5.6.3
to
h(t,x,v)=Z^{\beta}f(t,x,v)
and then Proposition
5.4.1
, we obtain the following estimates.

Corollary 5.6.4
.

For any
|\beta|\leq N
and all
(t,x)\in[0,T)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
, we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}\big|Z^{\beta}f\big|(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\dfrac{\epsilon\,\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{4}}}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}.

If
C_{\mathrm{boot}}
is chosen large enough, Corollary
5.6.4
improves the bootstrap assumption
(BA2)
and concludes the proof of the global existence part of Theorem
3.1
.

6.
Modified scattering

We follow here the strategy outlined in Section
1.4.1
. We consider a global solution
f
to (
VP
) arising from data satisfying the assumptions of Theorem
3.1
. Although all the results of this section could be adapted to the derivatives of order
N-1
, we restrict our analysis to the lower order derivatives for convenience. We assume here that
N\geq 2
.

6.1.
Convergence of the spatial averages

Recall
g_{0}(t,x,v):=f(t,x+tv,v)
. The first step consists in proving the next result.

Proposition 6.1.1
.

There exists
Q_{\infty}\in C^{N-2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
such that, for any
|\beta|\leq N-2
, we have

\forall\,(t,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},\qquad\langle v\rangle^{4}\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta}g_{0}(t,x,v){\mathrm{d}}x-\partial_{v}^{\beta}Q_{\infty}(v)\bigg|\lesssim\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-1}\log^{N-1}(t).

Proof.

As
[G^{\beta}f](t,x+tv,v)=\partial_{v}^{\beta}g_{0}(t,x,v)
, the decay estimate in Proposition
5.5.1
implies

\langle v\rangle^{4}\bigg|\partial_{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\beta}g_{0}(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x\bigg|\lesssim\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-2}\sup_{|\kappa|\leq|\beta|+1}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}\,\langle v\rangle^{4}\big|\mathbf{z}^{4}_{\mathrm{mod}}Z^{\kappa}f(t,x,v)\big|\lesssim\epsilon\langle t\rangle^{-2}\log^{N-1}(t),

where we used Proposition
5.4.1
in the last step. This implies the result.
∎

6.2.
Leading contribution of the spatial density

Next, we prove that
t^{3}\rho(G^{\beta}f)
has an asymptotic self-similar profile for any
|\beta|\leq N-2
. For this, we first apply Propositions
5.6.1
and
6.1.1
, and then remark that
t^{3}\,\langle x/t\rangle^{3}\gtrsim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}
for
t\geq 2
.

Proposition 6.2.1
.

Let
|\beta|\leq N-2
. Then, for all
(t,x)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
, we have

\Big|t^{3}\rho\big(G^{\beta}f\big)(t,x)-\partial_{v}^{\beta}Q_{\infty}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\Big|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\,t^{2}\log^{N}(t)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}.

Remark 6.2.1
.

Proposition
6.2.1
identifies the leading order contribution of the spatial derivatives of
\rho(f)
since
t^{3+|\beta|}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\rho(f)=t^{3}\rho(G^{\beta}f)
.

6.3.
Asymptotic behaviour of the force field

We are then able to derive the asymptotic self-similar behaviour of the force field and its derivatives. For this, we exploit the precise asymptotic behaviour of
\rho(f)
.

Proposition 6.3.1
.

Let
\phi_{\infty}\in C^{N-2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
be defined as

\Delta_{v}\phi_{\infty}=Q_{\infty}.

Then, for any
|\kappa|\leq N-2
, we have

\forall\,(t,x)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x},\qquad\Big|t^{2+|\kappa|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi(t,x)-\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\phi_{\infty}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\Big|\lesssim\epsilon\,\frac{\log^{N}(t)}{t}.

Moreover, we have
\|\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\|_{W^{N-2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})}\lesssim\epsilon
.

Proof.

Let
|\kappa|\leq N-2
, and set
\psi^{\kappa}(t,x):=\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\phi_{\infty}(\frac{x}{t})
. Then,
t^{2}\Delta_{x}\psi^{\kappa}=\partial_{v}^{\kappa}Q_{\infty}(\frac{x}{t})
, so

t^{2}\Delta_{x}\big[t^{1+|\kappa|}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi-\psi^{\kappa}\big](t,x)=t^{3}\rho\big(G^{\kappa}f\big)(t,x)-\partial_{v}^{\kappa}Q_{\infty}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big).

According to Proposition
6.2.1
, this quantity is bounded by
\epsilon\,t^{2}\log^{N}(t)\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-3}
. It remains to use the estimate (
15
).
∎

We are then able to describe the asymptotic behaviour of
t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi
along the spatial characteristics of the linearised system
t\mapsto x+tv
.

Corollary 6.3.2
.

Let
|\kappa|\leq N-2
. Then, for all
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\big|t^{2+|\kappa|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi(t,x+tv)-\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\phi_{\infty}(v)\big|\lesssim\epsilon\log^{N}(t)t^{-1}+\epsilon|x|t^{-1}.

Proof.

By the mean value theorem and the estimates in Proposition
5.2.2
, we have

t^{2+|\kappa|}\big|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi(t,x+tv)-\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi(t,tv)\big|\lesssim\epsilon|x|t^{-1}.

It remains to apply Proposition
6.3.1
.
∎

6.4.
Modified scattering for the distribution function

Now that we have identified the term responsible for the long-range effect of the force field, that is, the term preventing linear scattering to hold, we define the spatial modification of the characteristics as

{\mathbf{X}}_{1}(t,x,v):=x+\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v).

The modification
{\mathbf{X}}_{1}
is a good approximation of the spatial nonlinear characteristics, as it can be observed in Lemma
7.1.4
below. Then, as
{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(f)=0
, we have

\partial_{t}\big[f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv,v)\big]=\partial_{t}\big[{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\big]\cdot\nabla_{x}f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv,v)+\mu\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv)\cdot\nabla_{v}f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv,v),

where we have dropped the dependence in
(t,x,v)
of
{\mathbf{X}}_{1}
. Writing
\nabla_{v}=G-t\nabla_{x}
yields

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\big[f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv,v)\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu}{t}\Big(\!\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)-t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv)\!\Big)\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}f\big](t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv,v)

\displaystyle\quad+\mu\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv)\cdot\big[Gf\big](t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv,v).

Then, we need to determine the asymptotic behaviour of the force field along the modified spatial characteristics. Since the corrections are lower order, it sufficies to apply Corollary
6.3.2
for
x={\mathbf{X}}_{1}
. As
|\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)|\lesssim\epsilon
, we obtain
|{\mathbf{X}}_{1}|\leq|x|+\log(t)
, so that

(27)

\big|\partial_{t}\big[f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv,v)\big]\big|\lesssim\epsilon\,t^{-2}\big(\log^{N}(t)+\langle x\rangle\big)\big|\nabla_{x}f\big|(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv,v)+\epsilon t^{-2}\big|Gf\big|(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv,v).

We remark now that according to Corollary
5.4.2
, for any
Z\in\boldsymbol{\lambda}
we have

(28)

\langle x\rangle^{8}\,\langle v\rangle^{7}\big|Zf\big|(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv,v)\lesssim\big(\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\rangle^{8}+\log^{8}(t)\big)\,\langle v\rangle^{7}\big|\nabla_{x,v}g_{0}\big|(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1},v)\lesssim\epsilon\log^{9}(t).

Thus, we have

\langle x\rangle^{7}\,\langle v\rangle^{7}|\partial_{t}[f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv,v)]|\lesssim\epsilon^{2}t^{-2}\log^{N+9}(t),

from which we deduce that
f
enjoys a modified scattering dynamic.

Proposition 6.4.1
.

There exists a distribution function
\widetilde{f}_{\infty}\in C^{N-2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
such that for every
|\kappa|\leq N-2
and all
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\langle x\rangle^{7}\langle v\rangle^{7}\Big|\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\big[f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}(t,x,v)+tv,v)\big]-\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\widetilde{f}_{\infty}(x,v)\Big|\lesssim\epsilon t^{-1}\log^{2N+8}(t).

Remark 6.4.1
.

By a slightly more precise analysis, one could obtain from (
27
) and Corollary
5.4.2
that
|f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}(t,x,v)+tv,v)-\widetilde{f}_{\infty}|\lesssim\epsilon t^{-1}\log^{2}(t)
as stated in Theorem
1.1
.

So far, we have only proved the convergence result in a weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
norm. For the purpose of deriving late-time asymptotics, we will require a similar result but for different modified characteristics. In order to avoid repetitions, we postpone the proof of the high order regularity statement, which can be obtained, either by following the proof of Proposition
7.1.5
and by formally replacing
{\mathbf{V}}_{1}
by
v
, or from (
30
) and
\|\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\|_{W^{N-2,\infty}_{v}}\lesssim\epsilon
.

7.
Improved expansion for the spatial density

The purpose of this section consists in deriving a first order expansion in powers of
t^{-1}
for the normalised spatial density
t^{3}\rho(f)
. We will also prove useful preparatory results for the proof of Theorem
3.2
, our main result.

In view of the discussion carried out in Section
1.4.2
, we introduce the modification

\mathbf{V}_{1}(t,z,v):=v+\mu t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)

and we set
g_{1}(t,z,v):=f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1})
, that is

g_{1}(t,z,v):=f\big(t,z+tv+\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)+\mu\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v),v+\mu t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)\big).

We first prove that
g_{1}
converges to

f_{\infty}(x,v)=\widetilde{f}_{\infty}(x+\mu\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v),v)

in a strong topology. In other words, we prove a modified scattering statement for
f
. Then, we prove an enhanced convergence estimate for the spatial average of
g_{1}
to the one of
f_{\infty}
. This will allow us to express
\rho(f)
in terms of
g_{1}
, and then obtain a first order expansion for
t^{3}\rho(f)
.

7.1.
Convergence properties of
g_{1}

Let us assume that
N\geq 2
. All the results derived here will require to compute the time derivative of
g_{1}(t,x,v)=f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1})
. Applying the chain rule, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g_{1}(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=\big[\partial_{t}f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big)+\big({\mathbf{V}}_{1}+t\partial_{t}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}+\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\big)\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\partial_{t}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\cdot\big[\nabla_{v}f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big).

We now rewrite
[\partial_{t}f](t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1})+{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\cdot[\nabla_{x}f](t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1})
using the Vlasov equation, and
\nabla_{v}=G-t\nabla_{x}
. We then obtain

(29)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g_{1}(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=\big(\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{1}-t\mu\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1})\big)\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\mu\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1})+\partial_{t}{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big)\cdot\big[Gf\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big).

Then, we determine the asymptotic behaviour of the force field along the modified spatial characteristics. Since the corrections are lower order, a result similar to Corollary
6.3.2
holds.

Corollary 7.1.1
.

Let
|\kappa|\leq N-2
. Then, for all
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\big|t^{2+|\kappa|}\big[\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa}\phi\big]\big(t,x+tv+\mu[\log(t)+1]\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)\big)-\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\phi_{\infty}(v)\big|\lesssim\epsilon\,\langle x\rangle\,\log^{N}(t)t^{-1}.

Proof.

We have
|x+\mu[\log(t)+1]\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)|\lesssim|x|+\log(t)
, so it suffices to apply Corollary
6.3.2
.
∎

More work is required to show that
g_{1}
converges in
W^{N-2,\infty}_{x,v}
. We next compute and control the derivatives of the error terms in (
29
).

Lemma 7.1.2
.

Let
|\kappa|\leq N-2
. For all
(t,x,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, the derivative
\partial_{t}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\big[f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1})\big]
can be written as a linear combination of terms of the form

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}\Big(\!\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)-t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1})\!\Big)\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G^{\beta}f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big)\frac{\log^{k}(t)}{t^{p-k}}\prod_{1\leq i\leq p}\partial_{v}^{\xi_{i}}\phi_{\infty}^{k_{i}}(v),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t^{2}}\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}\Big(\!\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)-t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1})\!\Big)\cdot\big[G\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G^{\beta}f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big)\frac{\log^{k}(t)}{t^{p-k}}\prod_{1\leq i\leq p}\partial_{v}^{\xi_{i}}\phi_{\infty}^{k_{i}}(v),

where
k\leq|\alpha|
,
0\leq k\leq p\leq|\kappa|
,
|\gamma|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq|\kappa|
,
2\leq|\xi_{i}|\leq|\kappa|+1
, and
k_{i}\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket
.

Remark 7.1.1
.

The condition
k\leq|\alpha|
is key to determine the asymptotic behaviour of the spatial average of the derivatives of
g_{1}
.

Proof.

We obtain the result from (
29
), and by iterating the relations

\displaystyle\partial_{x^{i}}\big[h(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1})\big]

\displaystyle=\big[\partial_{x^{i}}h\big](t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}),

\displaystyle\partial_{v^{i}}\big[h(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1})\big]

\displaystyle=\big[G_{i}h\big](t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1})+\frac{\mu}{t}\nabla_{v}\partial_{v^{i}}\phi_{\infty}(v)\cdot\big[Gh\big](t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\partial_{v^{i}}\phi_{\infty}(v)\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}h\big](t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}),

which hold for any distribution function
h:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\!\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}\to{\mathbb{R}}
.
∎

To show the convergence of the derivatives of
g_{1}
, we need the following result to control the terms in Lemma
7.1.2
.

Corollary 7.1.3
.

Let
|\kappa_{x}|\leq N-1
and
|\kappa_{v}|\leq N-2
. For all
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\big[\nabla_{x}\phi\big(t,x+tv+\mu[\log(t)+1]\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\big)\big]\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\,t^{-2-|\kappa_{x}|},

\displaystyle\big|t^{2}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}\big[\nabla_{x}\phi\big(t,x+tv+\mu[\log(t)+1]\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\big)\big]-\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}\phi_{\infty}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\,\langle x\rangle\log^{N}(t)t^{-1}.

Proof.

The first estimate follows from
\partial_{x^{i}}({\mathbf{X}}_{1}^{j})=\delta_{i}^{j}
and Proposition
5.2.2
. For the second estimate, we note that
\partial_{v^{i}}({\mathbf{X}}_{1}^{j}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1}^{j})=t\delta_{i,j}+\mu[\log(t)+1]\partial_{v^{i}}\partial_{v^{j}}\phi_{\infty}(v)
. Then, we write the difference

\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}\big[\nabla_{x}\phi\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big)\big]-t^{|\kappa_{v}|}\big[\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{v}}\phi\big](t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1})

as a linear combination of terms of the form

t^{|\alpha|-p}\big[\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\phi\big](t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1})\!\prod_{1\leq i\leq p}(\log(t)+1)\partial_{v}^{\xi_{i}}\phi_{\infty}(v),

with
1\leq p=|\alpha|\leq|\kappa_{v}|,
and
2\leq|\xi_{i}|\leq|\kappa_{v}|+1
. These last terms are bounded by
\epsilon^{1+p}t^{-3}\log^{p}(t)
, so the result follows from Corollary
7.1.1
.
∎

Finally, we prove that
{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+tv
, and then
{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1}
, are good approximations to the nonlinear spatial characteristics. The following lemma allows to save
N_{x}
powers of
\log(t)
in the error terms.

Lemma 7.1.4
.

For all
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\big|{\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}}(t,x,v)-\big\langle x-tv-\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)\big\rangle\big|\leq 4\,{\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}}(t,x,v).

Then, for any
|\kappa_{x}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N-1
and all
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{N_{x}}\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}G^{\kappa_{v}}f\big|\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}(t,x,v)+tv,v\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{|\kappa_{v}|}(t),

\displaystyle\langle x\rangle^{N_{x}}\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}G^{\kappa_{v}}f\big|\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}(t,x,v)+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1}(t,x,v),{\mathbf{V}}_{1}(t,x,v)\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{|\kappa_{v}|}(t).

Proof.

Let
z_{\mathrm{approx}}(t,x,v):=x-tv-\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)
. Then,

\displaystyle\big|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(z_{\mathrm{approx}})\big|(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\leq\big|t\nabla_{x}\phi(t,x)-t\nabla_{x}\phi(t,tv)+t\nabla_{x}\phi(t,tv)-t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)\big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\log(t)\big|\nabla_{x}\phi(t,x)\cdot\nabla_{v}^{2}\phi_{\infty}(v)\big|.

Thus, by the mean value theorem, and Propositions
5.2.2
and
6.3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\big|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(z_{\mathrm{approx}})\big|(t,x,v)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon t^{-2}|x-tv|+\epsilon t^{-2}\log^{N}(t)\lesssim\epsilon t^{-2}\big|z_{\mathrm{approx}}\big|(t,x,v)+\epsilon t^{-2}\log^{N}(t).

Denote by
\mathcal{F}(t,s,x,v)
the nonlinear characteristic flow induced by
{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}
. If
\epsilon
is small enough, for all
t\geq 2
we have

\langle z_{\mathrm{approx}}\rangle(t,\mathcal{F}(t,2,x,v))\leq 2\langle z_{\mathrm{approx}}\rangle\,(2,x,v)\leq 3\langle x-2v\rangle\leq 4{\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}}(2,x,v)=4{\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}}(t,\mathcal{F}(t,2,x,v)),

where, in the last step, we used that
{\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}}
is conserved along
\mathcal{F}
. We then obtain the desired estimate by applying Duhamel formula, since

|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}({\mathbf{z}_{\mathrm{mod}}}-\langle z_{\mathrm{approx}}\rangle)|\leq|{\mathbf{T}}_{\phi}(z_{\mathrm{approx}})|.

The first estimate for
f
then follows from Proposition
5.4.1
. Moreover, the second estimate holds since
|v-{\mathbf{V}}_{1}(t,x,v)|\lesssim\epsilon t^{-1}
.
∎

We are now able to prove a modified scattering result for
f
in a strong topology. Recall for this that
g_{1}(t,x,v)=f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1})
.

Proposition 7.1.5
.

There exists a scattering state
f_{\infty}\in C^{N-2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
such that the modified profile
g_{1}
converges to
f_{\infty}
. More precisely, for any
|\kappa|\leq N-2
and all
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\langle x\rangle^{N_{x}-1}\,\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\Big|\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}g_{1}(t,x,v)-\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}f_{\infty}(x,v)\Big|\lesssim\frac{\log^{2N}(t)}{t}.

Remark 7.1.2
.

If
(N_{x},N_{v})=(8,7)
, then the RHS could be improved to
\epsilon t^{-1}\log^{2N}(t)
.

Proof.

Lemma
7.1.2
and Corollary
7.1.3
imply that for any
|\kappa|\leq N-2
and all
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\big|\partial_{t}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\big[f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1})\big]\big|\lesssim\epsilon\log^{N+|\alpha|}(t)t^{-2}\,\langle x\rangle\sup_{|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N-1}\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G^{\beta}f\big|(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}).

We then deduce from Lemma
7.1.4
that, for any
|\kappa|\leq N-2
and all
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
,

(30)

\langle x\rangle^{N_{x}-1}\,\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\big|\partial_{t}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\big[f(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1})\big]\big|\lesssim\log^{2N-1}(t)t^{-2},

This estimate implies that
g_{1}
converges to a
C^{N-2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
function with the stated rate of convergence.
∎

Remark 7.1.3
.

As
|{\mathbf{V}}_{1}-v|=\mu t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)
, we have
f_{\infty}(x,v)=\widetilde{f}_{\infty}(x+\mu\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v),v)
by the mean value theorem. Here,
\widetilde{f}_{\infty}
is the limit function in Proposition
6.4.1
.

We now derive some direct consequences of Proposition
7.1.5
that we will use below. In particular, we emphasise that the weighted spatial averages of
g_{1}
converge, contrary to the ones of
g_{0}
.

Corollary 7.1.6
.

Let
|\kappa|\leq N-2
. There exists
C>0
such that for all
(x,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
and all
t\in[2,\infty]
, we have

\langle x\rangle^{N_{x}-1}\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}|\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}g_{1}|(t,x,v)\leq C,

where we set
g_{1}(\infty,x,v):=f_{\infty}(x,v)
. As a result, for all
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
and all
t\in[2,\infty]
, we have

\langle v\rangle^{3}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}\langle z\rangle^{N_{x}-5}\,|\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}g_{1}|(t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z\lesssim C.

Finally, for any
|\xi|\leq N_{x}-5
, we have

\forall\,(t,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},\qquad\langle v\rangle^{3}\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}z^{\xi}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}g_{1}(t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}z^{\xi}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}f_{\infty}(z,v){\mathrm{d}}z\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\log^{2N}(t)}{t}.

Proof.

The first part of the statement is obtained by integrating (
30
) between
t=2
and
t\in[2,\infty]
, together with Lemma
7.1.4
, since

|\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}g_{1}(2,x,v)|\lesssim\sup_{|\alpha|\leq|\kappa|}\big|Z^{\alpha}f|\Big(2,x+2v+\mu[\log(2)+1]\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty},v+\frac{\mu}{2}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\Big).

We deduce the second estimate using
z\mapsto\langle z\rangle^{-4}\in L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z})
. Finally, the third estimate follows similarly from Proposition
7.1.5
.
∎

Before proving the expansion for
t^{3}\rho(f)
, we need to improve the rate of convergence of the spatial average of
\partial_{v}^{\kappa}g_{1}
. For this, we now require
N\geq 3
.

Proposition 7.1.7
.

For all
(t,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\langle v\rangle^{3}\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}g_{1}(t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}\Big[1-\mu\frac{1}{t}\Delta_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)\Big]f_{\infty}(z,v){\mathrm{d}}z\bigg|\lesssim\epsilon\frac{\log^{2N}(t)}{t^{2}}.

Moreover, for any
|\kappa|\leq N-3
there exist
n_{\beta,\gamma}^{\kappa}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

\displaystyle\langle v\rangle^{3}\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}\!\partial_{v}^{\kappa}g_{1}(t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z~-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}\!\partial_{v}^{\kappa}f_{\infty}(z,v){\mathrm{d}}z~

\displaystyle-\sum_{|\gamma|+|\beta|\leq|\kappa|}\!\frac{n_{\beta,\gamma}^{\kappa}}{t}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}\!\Delta_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\phi_{\infty}(v)\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\infty}(z,v){\mathrm{d}}z\bigg|\lesssim\epsilon t^{-2}\log^{2N}(t).

Proof.

Let
|\kappa|\leq N-3
and
(t,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
. Combining Lemma
7.1.2
, Corollary
7.1.3
, and Lemma
7.1.4
, we have

\displaystyle\langle v\rangle^{3}\bigg|\partial_{t}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}g_{1}(t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z\bigg)-\sum_{|\gamma|+|\beta|\leq|\kappa|}n_{\beta,\gamma}^{\kappa}\mathfrak{I}^{\kappa}_{\gamma,0,\beta}(t,v)\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\epsilon\frac{\log^{2N}(t)}{t^{3}}+\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|\leq|\kappa|}\;\sum_{|\gamma|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq|\kappa|}\log^{|\alpha|}(t)\langle v\rangle^{3}\big|\mathfrak{I}^{\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta}\big|(t,v),

where

\mathfrak{I}^{\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta}(t,v):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\frac{1}{t}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Big(\!\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)-t^{2}\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1})\!\Big)\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G^{\beta}f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big){\mathrm{d}}x.

Since
{\mathbf{X}}_{1}-x
,
{\mathbf{V}}_{1}
, and
\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{1}
, do not depend on
x
, we show by integration by parts that

\mathfrak{I}^{\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta}(t,v)=t\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\Big(\Delta_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1})\Big)\big[G^{\beta}f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big){\mathrm{d}}x

do not depend on
x
either. Thus, we have

\log^{|\alpha|}(t)\langle v\rangle^{3}\big|\mathfrak{I}^{\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta}\big|(t,v)\lesssim\epsilon\log^{2N}(t)t^{-2-|\alpha|},

by Lemma
7.1.4
and Corollary
7.1.3
. However, for
|\alpha|=0
the term
\mathfrak{I}^{\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta}
does not decay faster than
t^{-2}
. Nonetheless, we have identified the leading order contribution of
\Delta_{x}\phi
, so we can find a correction to the spatial average of
g_{1}
allowing for a stronger rate of convergence.

We note first that by performing similar computations as in the proof of Corollary
7.1.3
, we obtain

\Big|\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Big(\Delta_{x}\phi\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big)\Big)-t^{|\gamma|}\big[\Delta_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big)\Big|\lesssim\epsilon\log^{|\gamma|}(t)t^{-4}.

We then deduce, by peforming first the change of variables
z=x+tv+\mu[\log(t)+1]\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)
and using Lemma
7.1.4
that

\displaystyle\langle v\rangle^{3}

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathfrak{I}^{\kappa}_{\gamma,0,\beta}-\frac{1}{t^{2}}\Delta_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\phi_{\infty}(v)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}\big[G^{\beta}f\big]\big(t,z,{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big){\mathrm{d}}z\bigg|

\displaystyle\;\;\lesssim\epsilon\frac{\log^{2N}(t)}{t^{3}}+t\langle v\rangle^{3}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\Big|\big[\Delta_{x}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\big]\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1})-\frac{1}{t^{3}}\Delta_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\phi_{\infty}(v)\Big|\big|Z^{\beta}f\big|\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big){\mathrm{d}}x.

Finally, we bound the RHS of the previous inequality by
\epsilon\log^{2N}(t)t^{-3}
by applying Corollary
7.1.1
and Lemma
7.1.4
, as
|\gamma|\leq N-2
. One can then derive the result by using Proposition
6.1.1
. For the case
|\kappa|=0
, the coefficient
n^{\kappa}_{\gamma,\beta}
is given by (
29
).
∎

7.2.
First order expansion of
t^{3}\rho(f)

In this subsection, we assume
N\geq 4
. As we need to express
\rho(f)
using the distribution
g_{1}
, we have to study the maps

\Psi_{t}\colon(z,v)\mapsto\big(z+\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v),v+\mu t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)\big),\qquad\Upsilon_{t}\colon(a,b)\mapsto(a+tb,b),

for
t\geq 2
. In terms of these maps, we have

g_{1}(t,\cdot,\cdot)=f(t,\Upsilon_{t}\circ\Psi_{t}).

For our purposes, we need to show that
\Psi_{t}
is invertible and consider

(31)

f(t,z,v)=g_{1}\big(t,\Psi_{t}^{-1}(z-tv,v)\big)=g_{1}(t,z-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}(v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}(v)\big),

where
(\mathfrak{X}_{t},\mathfrak{V}_{t}):=\Psi_{t}^{-1}-\mathrm{id}
. In the following, we denote the space of matrices of size
d\times d
with real coefficients by
\mathfrak{M}_{d}({\mathbb{R}})
. We begin proving a preparatory result.

Lemma 7.2.1
.

Let
d\geq 1
and
k\geq 1
. Let
h\in C^{k}({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
be a map verifying
\|h\|_{W^{k,\infty}}\!<\!\infty
, and
\|{\mathrm{d}}h\|_{L^{\infty}}<1
. Then, the map
H:=\mathrm{id}-h
is a
C^{k}
-diffeomorphism of
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, and

(32)

\big[{\mathrm{d}}H\big]^{-1}=\mathrm{id}+\sum_{i\geq 1}\big[{\mathrm{d}}h\big]^{k}\quad\text{in $C^{k-1}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{d},\mathfrak{M}_{d}({\mathbb{R}})\big)$},

where
[{\mathrm{d}}h\big]
is the Jacobian matrix of
h
, and
[{\mathrm{d}}h]_{ij}:=\partial_{x^{j}}h^{i}
for all
1\leq i,\,j\leq d
.

Proof.

For the first part of the statement, we have three steps:

•

H
is injective. This follows from the inequality

\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\square\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\qquad\big||x-y|-|H(x)-H(y)|\big|\!\leq\big|x-y-H(x)+H(y)\big|\leq|h(x)-h(y)|\leq\|{\mathrm{d}}h\|_{L^{\infty}}|x-y|<|x-y|,

which relies on the mean value theorem.

•

\mathrm{d}H
is a local
C^{k}
diffeomorphism. As
\|{\mathrm{d}}h\|_{L^{\infty}}<1
, the map
{\mathrm{d}}H=\mathrm{id}-{\mathrm{d}}h
is invertible for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, so (
32
) holds in
C^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{d},\mathfrak{M}_{d}({\mathbb{R}}))
. (
32
) holds as well in high regularity as
\|{\mathrm{d}}h\|_{W^{k-1,\infty}}<\infty
.

•

H
is surjective. Let
(x_{n})_{n\geq 0}
be a sequence in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
such that
H(x_{n})\to y_{\infty}
as
n\to\infty
, with
y_{\infty}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
. Since
H-\mathrm{id}\in L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d},{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
, we obtain that
(x_{n})_{n\geq 0}
is bounded. By the Bolzano-Weierstrass theorem and by continuity, there exists
x_{\infty}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
such that
H(x_{\infty})=y_{\infty}
. As
H({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
is open by the previous step, it yields
H({\mathbb{R}}^{d})={\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.

The second part of the statement is a direct consequence of the first one.
∎

We are now able to study
\Psi_{t}
. In particular, we study fine properties of the components of
\Psi_{t}^{-1}-\mathrm{id}=(\mathfrak{X}_{t},\mathfrak{V}_{t})
, which will allow us to estimate
f
by (
31
).

Lemma 7.2.2
.

For all
t\geq 2
, the map
\Psi_{t}
is a
C^{N-2}
-diffeomorphism of
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
. The spatial and velocity components of
\Psi_{t}^{-1}-\mathrm{id}=(\mathfrak{X}_{t},\mathfrak{V}_{t})
are independent of the spatial variable. Moreover, these components verify for all
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{X}_{t}(v)

\displaystyle=-\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)+\sum_{1\leq k\leq N-3}t^{-k}\log(t)\phi_{k}^{X}(v)+\epsilon^{N-1}\,O\big(t^{-N+2}\log(t)\big),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{V}_{t}(v)

\displaystyle=-\mu t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)+\sum_{2\leq k\leq N-2}t^{-k}\phi^{V}_{k}(v)+\epsilon^{N-1}O\big(t^{-N+1}\big),

where
\phi_{k-1}^{X},\,\phi_{k}^{V}\in C^{N-1-k}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
, and their components are linear combination of terms of the form

\prod_{1\leq i\leq k}\partial_{v}^{\gamma_{i}}\phi_{\infty}^{k_{i}}(v),\qquad 1\leq|\gamma_{i}|\leq k,\quad k_{i}\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket.

Moreover, for any
|\kappa|\leq N-3
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\mathfrak{X}_{t}(v)

\displaystyle=-\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\phi_{\infty}(v)+\sum_{1\leq k\leq N-3-|\kappa|}t^{-k}\log(t)\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\phi_{k}^{X}(v)+\epsilon^{N-1-|\kappa|}\,O\big(t^{-N+2+|\kappa|}\log(t)\big),

\displaystyle\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\mathfrak{V}_{t}(v)

\displaystyle=-\mu t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\phi_{\infty}(v)+\sum_{2\leq k\leq N-2-|\kappa|}t^{-k}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\phi^{V}_{k}(v)+\epsilon^{N-1-|\kappa|}O\big(t^{-N+1+|\kappa|}\big).

Finally,
\|\mathfrak{X}\|_{\dot{W}^{N-2,\infty}}\lesssim\epsilon\log(t)
and
\|\mathfrak{V}\|_{\dot{W}^{N-2,\infty}}\lesssim t^{-1}\epsilon
.

Proof.

We fix
t\geq 2
and decompose

\Psi_{t}=\Phi_{t,1}\circ\Xi_{t},

where

\Phi_{t,1}(y,w)=\big(y,w+\mu t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(w)\big),\qquad\Xi_{t}(z,v)=\big(z+\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v),v\big).

Clearly,
\Xi_{t}
is a
C^{N-2}
diffeomorphism of
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
and

\Xi_{t}^{-1}(z,v)=\big(z-\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v),v\big).

Let us now prove that
\Phi_{t,1}
is a
C^{N-2}
-diffeomorphism. For this, it suffices to study its velocity component

\Omega_{t}:w\mapsto w+\mu t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(w).

As
\|\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\|_{W^{N-2,\infty}}\lesssim\epsilon
by Proposition
6.3.1
, we show by applying Lemma
7.2.1
that
\Omega_{t}
is a
C^{N-2}
-diffeomorphism, and

(33)

[{\mathrm{d}}\Omega_{t}]^{-1}=\mathrm{id}+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,[{\mathrm{d}}h_{t}]^{k}\quad\text{in $C^{N-3}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},\mathfrak{M}_{3}({\mathbb{R}})\big)$},\qquad[{\mathrm{d}}h_{t}]_{ij}=-\mu t^{-1}\partial_{v^{i}}\partial_{v^{j}}\phi_{\infty},

where
h_{t}(w):=-\mu t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(w)
. Thus,
\Psi_{t}
is a
C^{N-2}
-diffeomorphism, and

(34)

\Psi_{t}^{-1}(z,v)=\big(z-\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\big(\Omega_{t}^{-1}(v)\big),\Omega_{t}^{-1}(v)\big).

The next step consists in deriving an expansion for
\Omega_{t}^{-1}
. Let
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
and
w=\Omega_{t}^{-1}(v)
, so that
v=w+\mu t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(w)
. Then, by applying the mean value theorem we have

|v-w|\lesssim\epsilon t^{-1},\qquad\big|v-\mu t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)-w\big|\lesssim t^{-1}\big\|\nabla_{v}^{2}\phi_{\infty}\big\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}|v-w|\lesssim\epsilon^{2}t^{-2},

as
\|\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\|_{W^{N-2,\infty}}\lesssim\epsilon
. Iterating the above, and using Taylor’s theorem instead of the mean value theorem, we obtain

(35)

\mathfrak{V}_{t}(v)=\Omega_{t}^{-1}(v)-v=-\mu t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)+\sum_{2\leq k\leq N-2}t^{-k}\phi^{V}_{k}(v)+\epsilon^{N-1}O\big(t^{-N+1}\big),

where
\phi^{V}_{k}\in C^{N-1-k}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
. In fact,
\phi_{k}^{V}
is a product of
k
derivatives of
\partial_{v^{i}}\phi_{\infty}
. Together with the estimate
\|\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\|_{W^{N-2,\infty}}\lesssim\epsilon
and (
34
), we obtain the expansion for
\mathfrak{X}_{t}
. We next remark that

{\mathrm{d}}\Psi_{t}^{-1}(z,v)={\mathrm{d}}\Xi_{t}^{-1}\big(z,\Omega_{t}^{-1}(v)\big)\circ{\mathrm{d}}\Phi_{t,1}^{-1}(z,v),\qquad{\mathrm{d}}\Phi^{-1}_{t,1}(z,v)\cdot(h,k)=\big(h,\big[{\mathrm{d}}\Omega_{t}\big]^{-1}\big(\Omega_{t}^{-1}(v)\big)\cdot k\big),

where
(h,k)
is a tangent vector in
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
. The existence of the expansions for the derivatives of
(\mathfrak{X}_{t},\mathfrak{V}_{t})
then ensue from an induction, (
33
), (
35
), Leibniz rule, and Taylor’s theorem. Lemma
2.6.3
finally shows that the coefficients of these time expansions are the derivatives of the ones for
(\mathfrak{X}_{t},\mathfrak{V}_{t})
.
∎

The next step consists in performing the change of variables
y=x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}(v)
for fixed
x
. For this purpose, we study the map
v\mapsto z-tv+\mathfrak{X}(v)
, and its inverse.

Lemma 7.2.3
.

Let
t\geq 2
and
z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}
. The map
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,z}:v\mapsto z-tv+\mathfrak{X}(v)
is a
C^{N-2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
-diffeomorphism. Moreover, the inverse
{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}_{t,z}
satisfies that for all
y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, we have

(36)

{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}_{t,z}(y)=\frac{z-y}{t}-\mu\frac{\log(t)}{t}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\Big(\frac{z}{t}\Big)+\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\leq q\leq N-2\\
|\alpha|+p\leq q\end{subarray}}\frac{y^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\mathbf{A}_{q,\alpha,p}\Big(\frac{z}{t}\Big)+\epsilon\,O\bigg(\frac{\langle y\rangle^{N-2}\log^{N-1}(t)}{t^{N-1}}\bigg),

where
\mathbf{A}_{q,\alpha,p}\in C^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
and
\|\mathbf{A}_{q,\alpha,p}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\epsilon^{1+p}
. The Jacobian determinant satisfies

(37)

t^{3}\big|\det\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,z}^{-1}\big|(y)=1-\mu\frac{\log(t)}{t}\Delta_{v}\phi_{\infty}\Big(\frac{z}{t}\Big)+\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\leq q\leq N-3\\
|\alpha|+p\leq q\end{subarray}}\frac{y^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\mathbf{B}_{q,\alpha,p}\Big(\frac{z}{t}\Big)+\epsilon\,O\bigg(\frac{\langle y\rangle^{N-3}\log^{N-2}(t)}{t^{N-2}}\bigg),

where
\mathbf{B}_{q,\alpha,p}\in C^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
and
\|\mathbf{B}_{q,\alpha,p}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\epsilon^{1+p}
. Finally, we have

(38)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}_{t,z}(y)+\mathfrak{V}_{t}\circ{\mathbf{Y}}^{-1}_{t,z}(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{z}{t}-\frac{y}{t}-\mu\frac{\log(t)+1}{t}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\Big(\frac{z}{t}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\!\sum_{2\leq q\leq N-2}\,\sum_{|\alpha|+p\leq q}\frac{y^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\mathbf{\overline{A}}_{q,\alpha,p}\Big(\frac{z}{t}\Big)+\epsilon\,O\bigg(\frac{\langle y\rangle^{N-2}\log^{N-1}(t)}{t^{N-1}}\bigg),

where
\mathbf{\overline{A}}_{q,\alpha,p}\in C^{0}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
and
\|\mathbf{\overline{A}}_{q,\alpha,p}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\epsilon^{1+p}
.

Proof.

Let
t\geq 2
and
z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
. The analysis can be reduced to the study of

(39)

\mathfrak{Z}(v):=v-t^{-1}\mathfrak{X}_{t}(v),\qquad\det{\mathrm{d}}{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,z}(v)=-t^{3}\det{\mathrm{d}}\mathfrak{Z}(v).

Note that
y={\mathbf{Y}}_{t,z}(v)
if and only if

\mathfrak{Z}(v)=\frac{z-y}{t}.

We next show estimates for
\mathfrak{Z}
from where the stated estimates (
36
)–(
38
) are obtained. Applying Lemma
7.2.1
with
(H,h)=(\mathfrak{Z},t^{-1}\mathfrak{X})
, together with Lemma
7.2.2
, we get that
\mathfrak{Z}
is a
C^{N-2}
-diffeomorphism of
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
and

\big[{\mathrm{d}}\mathfrak{Z}\big]^{-1}=\mathrm{id}+\sum_{k\geq 1}t^{-k}\big[{\mathrm{d}}\mathfrak{X}_{t}\big]^{k}\quad\text{in $C^{N-3}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{3},\mathfrak{M}_{3}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})\big)$},

where
[{\mathrm{d}}\mathfrak{X}_{t}]_{ij}=\partial_{v^{j}}\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{i}
. We now have to derive an expansion for
\mathfrak{Z}
. The strategy is similar to the one for
\Omega_{t}^{-1}
. Let
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
and
w=\mathfrak{Z}^{-1}(v)
, so that
v=w-t^{-1}\mathfrak{X}_{t}(w)
. Using
\|\mathfrak{X}\|_{W^{N-2,\infty}}\lesssim\epsilon\log(t)
, we then get
|v-w|\lesssim\epsilon t^{-1}\log(t)
. By an induction, Taylor’s theorem, and the expansion for
\mathfrak{X}_{t}
given by Lemma
7.2.2
, we have

(40)

\mathfrak{Z}^{-1}(v)=v-\mu\frac{\log(t)}{t}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)+\sum_{2\leq k\leq N-2}\frac{\log^{k}(t)}{t^{k}}\psi_{k}(v)+\epsilon^{N-1}O\bigg(\frac{\log^{N-1}(t)}{t^{N-1}}\bigg),

where
\psi_{k}\in C^{N-1-k}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\mathbb{R}}^{3})
and
\|\psi_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\epsilon^{k}
. Two other applications of Taylor’s theorem, with one of them relying on (
40
) and the expansion for
\mathfrak{V}
given in Lemma
7.2.2
, provide the estimates for
\mathfrak{Z}^{-1}(\frac{z-y}{t})
and
\mathfrak{Z}^{-1}(\frac{z-y}{t})+\mathfrak{V}_{t}\circ\mathfrak{Z}^{-1}(\frac{z-y}{t})
, which allow to show (
36
) and (
38
). The expansion (
37
) for the Jacobian determinant of
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,z}
follows from (
39
)–(
40
), Lemma
7.2.2
, the multi-linearity of the determinant, and

{\mathrm{d}}\mathfrak{Z}^{-1}(y)=\big[{\mathrm{d}}\mathfrak{Z}\big]^{-1}\big(\mathfrak{Z}^{-1}(y)\big),\qquad\det(I_{3}+M)=1+\mathrm{Tr}(M)+O(|M|^{2}).

∎

We are now able to derive an improved expansion for the spatial density. The improved expansion for derivatives of the spatial density will be handled in the next section. We apply the previous results with
N=4
.

Proposition 7.2.4
.

For all
(t,x)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
, there holds

\displaystyle t^{3}\rho(f)(t,x)=

\displaystyle\bigg[1-\mu\frac{\log(t)+1}{t}\Delta_{v}\phi_{\infty}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\bigg]\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}f_{\infty}\Big(y,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}\Big[y+\mu\big[\log(t)+1\big]\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\Big]\cdot\big[\nabla_{v}f_{\infty}\big]\Big(y,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y+\epsilon\,O\bigg(\frac{\log^{8}(t)}{t^{2}}\bigg).

Proof.

Let
(t,x)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
. Performing the change of variables
y={\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}(v)
, allowed by Lemma
7.2.3
, we have

\displaystyle\rho(f)(t,x)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}g_{1}\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}(v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}(v)\big){\mathrm{d}}v=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}g_{1}\Big(t,{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}(v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}(v)\Big){\mathrm{d}}v

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}g_{1}\Big(t,y,{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{-1}(y)+\mathfrak{V}_{t}\big({\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{-1}(y)\big)\Big)\big|\det{\mathrm{d}}{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{-1}\big|(y){\mathrm{d}}y.

We now apply Taylor’s theorem by using the expansion of Lemma
7.2.3
up to first order. This argument yields, by using the estimates of Corollary
7.1.6
for
\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g_{1}
with
|\kappa_{v}|\leq 2
, that

\displaystyle t^{3}\rho(f)(t,x)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}g_{1}\Big(t,y,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y-\mu\frac{\log(t)}{t}\Delta_{v}\phi_{\infty}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}g_{1}\Big(t,y,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y-\frac{1}{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}y\cdot\big[\nabla_{v}g_{1}\big]\Big(t,y,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y

\displaystyle\qquad-\mu\frac{\log(t)+1}{t}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\cdot\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}\big[\nabla_{v}g_{1}\big]\Big(t,y,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y+\epsilon\,O\bigg(\frac{\log^{2}(t)}{t^{2}}\bigg).

Finally, we treat the terms on the RHS by applying Proposition
7.1.7
for the first term, and Corollary
7.1.6
for the remaining ones.
∎

8.
Late-time asymptotics

During this section, we set
N\geq 3
,
N_{v}\geq 7
and
N_{x}\geq 2\sqrt{2N}+5
. We note that
N_{x}\geq 2n+8
if
n
satisfies
r_{n+1}\leq N
. We consider further a solution
f
to the Vlasov-Poisson system verifying
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{8,7}[f_{0}]\leq\epsilon
and
\mathbb{E}_{N}^{N_{x},N_{v}}[f_{0}]<\infty
.

8.1.
Enhanced modified characteristics and asymptotics

The purpose of this section consists in proving the properties stated in Proposition
8.1.1
by an induction argument. The base case
n=0
has been treated in Sections
6
–
7
. In the course of the proof, we will derive the asymptotic self-similar polyhomogeneous expansions satisfied by the normalised spatial density
t^{3}\!\rho(G^{\beta}\!f)
and normalised force field
t^{2+|\beta|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\phi
.

For convenience, we will use two sequences
(r_{n})_{n\geq 1}
and
(S_{n})_{n\geq 1}
which satisfy the following conditions

\displaystyle r_{n+1}

\displaystyle=r_{n}+n+1,\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle r_{1}=2,

\displaystyle S_{n+1}

\displaystyle\geq S_{n}+N+1-r_{n+1},\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle S_{1}\geq 2N.

The sequence
(r_{n})
, already introduced in (
1
), will express the number of derivatives required to derive expansions of order n for the solutions of the system. The sequence
(S_{n})
will express the logarithmical growth in the error terms at order
n
. One can check that a possible choice for
(S_{n})
is
S_{n}=N(n+2)
.

Proposition 8.1.1
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
r_{n}\leq N
,
{\mathbf{X}}_{0}(t,x,v):=x,
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{0}(t,x,v):=v
. There exist modifications of the linear characteristic flow

(41)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{X}}_{n}(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=x+\mu\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)\log(t)+\sum_{1\leq q\leq n-1}\,\sum_{|\alpha|+p\leq q}\frac{x^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\mathbb{X}_{q,\alpha,p}(v),

(42)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{V}}_{n}(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=v+\frac{\mu}{t}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)+\sum_{1\leq q\leq n-1}\,\sum_{|\alpha|+p\leq q}\frac{x^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q+1}}\mathbb{V}_{q,\alpha,p}(v),

where
\mathbb{X}_{1,\alpha,0}=-\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\alpha}\phi_{\infty}
for
|\alpha|=1
, and
\mathbb{X}_{q,\alpha,p},\;\mathbb{V}_{q,\alpha,p}\in C^{N-r_{q+1}}\cap W^{N-r_{q+1}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
, such that the following properties hold. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, if
r_{n+1}\leq N
and
|\beta|\leq N-r_{n+1}
:

(a)

Modified scattering holds with an enhanced rate of convergence. Let
g_{n+1}:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be defined by

g_{n+1}(t,x,v):=f\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}(t,x,v)+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}(t,x,v),{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}(t,x,v)\big).

For any
|\kappa|\leq N-r_{n+1}
and all
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
such that
|x|\leq t
, we have

(43)

\langle x\rangle^{N_{x}-1-n}\,\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\big|\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}\big(g_{n+1}(t,x,v)-f_{\infty}(x,v)\big)\big|\lesssim t^{-n-1}\log^{S_{n+1}}(t).

(b)

The modified spatial average verifies an enhanced convergence estimate to the spatial average of
f_{\infty}
. For any
|\kappa|\leq N-1-r_{n+1}
,
there exists
\mathbf{Q}_{p,\xi}^{\kappa,\beta}\in C^{0}\cap L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
such that

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\square\qquad\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bigg|\int_{|x|<t}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}g_{n+1}(t,x,v){\mathrm{d}}x-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}f_{\infty}(x,v){\mathrm{d}}x-\sum_{|\beta|\leq|\kappa|}

\displaystyle\sum_{p+|\xi|\leq n}\frac{\log^{p}(t)}{t^{n+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{p,\xi}^{\kappa,\beta}(v)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}x^{\xi}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\infty}(x,v){\mathrm{d}}x\bigg|

(44)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log^{S_{n+1}}(t)}{t^{n+2}}

\displaystyle,\qquad\forall(t,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}.

Remark 8.1.1
.

The domain of integration of the spatial average in (
44
) is restricted to
|x|\leq t
since
x\mapsto{\mathbf{X}}_{n}(t,x,v)+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n}(t,x,v)
may not be injective on
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
for
n\geq 2
. Note that
f(t,x+tv,v)
and its derivatives enjoy strong decay estimates on the complementary region
|x|\geq t
by Corollary
5.4.2
.

Structure of the proof of late-time asymptotics.

The strategy of the proof relies on an induction argument. Fix an integer
n
such that
r_{n+1}\leq N
. The base case
n=0
has been treated in Sections
6
–
7
. See in particular Propositions
6.2.1
,
6.3.1
,
7.1.5
and
7.1.7
. We then assume
n\geq 1
and that the statement holds at any order
k\in\llbracket 0,n-1\rrbracket
.

(1)

First, we derive the improved self-similar asymptotic expansion for
\rho(f)
and its derivatives in Section
8.2
. For this, we make use of the convergence properties of
g_{n}
,
Q_{n}
, and their derivatives.

(2)

The first step allows us to show an improved polyhomogeneous asymptotic expansion of the force field along the
n^{\mathrm{th}}
-order modifications of the linear characteristics in Section
8.3
.

(3)

In Section
8.4
, we define
{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}
from where the improved modified scattering statement hold. Introducing
{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}
is not required in this step but we do it to reduce the number of computations in the perspective of addressing part
(b)
of Proposition
8.1.1
.

(4)

Finally, we prove that the spatial average of
g_{n+1}
satisfies a strong convergence estimate in Section
8.5
. With this estimate, we complete the induction.

8.2.
Late-time asymptotics for the spatial density

We begin the proof of the late-time asymptotics for the spatial density by writing the distribution
G^{\beta}f
in terms of the modified profile
g_{n}
.

8.2.1.
Step 1:
G^{\beta}f
in terms of derivatives of
g_{n}

The asymptotic self-similar polyhomogeneous expansion for
t^{3}\rho(G^{\beta}f)
will be obtained through the convergence estimates satisfied by the weighted and non-weighted spatial averages of
\partial_{v}^{\kappa}g_{n}
. We then express
G^{\beta}f
in terms of
g_{n}
and its derivatives. For this, we recall that

g_{n}(t,x,v)=f\big({\mathbf{X}}_{n}(t,x,v)+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n}(t,x,v),{\mathbf{V}}_{n}(t,x,v)\big),

so we will have to invert the map
(x,v)\mapsto({\mathbf{X}}_{n}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n},{\mathbf{V}}_{n})
for all
t\geq 2
. As mentioned earlier, we will have to restrict our analysis to a subdomain of
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
.

To facilitate its study, we will write
({\mathbf{X}}_{n},{\mathbf{V}}_{n})
as the composition of three maps. Recall the definitions (
41
)–(
42
) of
({\mathbf{X}}_{n},{\mathbf{V}}_{n})
. We first rewrite (
41
)–(
42
) as

\displaystyle{\mathbf{X}}_{n}(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=x+\mu\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)\log(t)+\sum_{1\leq q\leq n-1}\,\sum_{|\alpha|+p\leq q}\frac{[x+\mu\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)\log(t)]^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\overline{\mathbb{X}}_{q,\alpha,p}(v),

\displaystyle{\mathbf{V}}_{n}(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=v+\frac{\mu}{t}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)+\sum_{1\leq q\leq n-1}\,\sum_{|\alpha|+p\leq q}\frac{[x+\mu\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)\log(t)]^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q+1}}\overline{\mathbb{V}}_{q,\alpha,p}(v),

where
\overline{\mathbb{X}}_{1,\alpha,0}=-\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\alpha}\phi_{\infty}
for
|\alpha|=1
, and
\overline{\mathbb{X}}_{q,\alpha,p},\;\overline{\mathbb{V}}_{q,\alpha,p}\in C^{N-r_{q+1}}\cap W^{N-r_{q+1}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
. It is the condition
|\alpha|+p\leq q
which allows
({\mathbf{X}}_{n},{\mathbf{V}}_{n})
to be written in this alternative form.

This leads us to consider the decomposition

({\mathbf{X}}_{n}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n},{\mathbf{V}}_{n})=\Upsilon_{t}\circ\Phi_{t,n}\circ\Xi_{t},

where we recall

\Upsilon_{t}(a,b):=(a+tb,b),\qquad\Xi_{t}(x,v):=\big(x+\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v),v\big),

and we define
\Phi_{t,n}
by

\Phi_{t,n}(y,w):=\mathrm{id}(y,w)+\big(\mathcal{X}^{n}_{t}(y,w),\mathcal{V}_{t}^{n}(y,w)\big),

with

\mathcal{X}^{n}_{t}(x,v):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq q\leq n-1\\
|\alpha|+p\leq q\end{subarray}}\frac{x^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\overline{\mathbb{X}}_{q,\alpha,p}(v),\qquad\mathcal{V}^{n}_{t}(x,v):=\frac{\mu}{t}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq q\leq n-1\\
|\alpha|+p\leq q\end{subarray}}\frac{x^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q+1}}\overline{\mathbb{V}}_{q,\alpha,p}(v).

Since
\Upsilon_{t}
and
\Xi_{t}
are diffeomorphisms of
{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
and that their inverse can be easily computed, we will focus on
\Phi_{t,n}
. We note that for all
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
such that
|x|\leq 2t
, we have

(45)

\big|\mathcal{X}_{t}^{n}\big|(t,x,v)\lesssim\frac{\epsilon|x|+\log(t)}{t}+\frac{|x|^{2}}{t^{2}},\qquad\big|\mathcal{V}_{t}^{n}\big|(t,x,v)\lesssim\frac{1}{t}.

The constant
\epsilon>0
in the estimate for
\mathcal{X}_{t}^{n}
comes from
\overline{\mathbb{X}}_{1,\alpha,0}=-\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\alpha}\phi_{\infty}
.

Lemma 8.2.1
.

There exists
T_{n}\!\geq\!2
and
0<\!c_{n}\!<1
such that for all
t\geq T_{n}
, there exists an open set
\mathcal{U}_{t,n}\subset\{|x|<2c_{n}t\}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
such that the restriction of
\Phi_{t,n}
to
\mathcal{U}_{t,n}
is a
C^{N-r_{n}}
-diffeomorphism from
\mathcal{U}_{t,n}
to
\{|x|<c_{n}t\}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
.

Proof.

We introduce the family of sets

\mathcal{A}_{t}^{c}:=\big\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\;:\;|x|<ct\big\}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},

where
0<c<2
. Then, by (
45
) there holds

\big\|\big(\mathcal{X}_{t}^{n},\mathcal{V}_{t}^{n}\big)\big\|_{W^{N-r_{n},\infty}(\mathcal{A}^{2c}_{t})}\lesssim\epsilon c+\log(t)t^{-1}+c^{2},

where
(\mathcal{X}_{t}^{n},\mathcal{V}_{t}^{n})\in C^{N-r_{n}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
. Thus, for
c<1
small enough and
t
large enough so that

(46)

\big\|\big(\mathcal{X}_{t}^{n},\mathcal{V}_{t}^{n}\big)\big\|_{W^{N-r_{n},\infty}(\mathcal{A}^{2c}_{t})}\leq\frac{1}{2}c,

the next properties hold by proceeding as in the proof of Lemma
7.2.1
:

•

\Phi_{t,n}
is injective on
\mathcal{A}^{2c}_{t}
.

•

\Phi_{t,n}
is a local
C^{N-r_{n}}
-diffeomorhism on
\mathcal{A}^{2c}_{t}
. Note that
N-r_{n}\geq 1
.

•

\mathcal{A}^{c}_{t}\subset\Phi_{t,n}(\mathcal{A}_{t}^{2c})
holds. We already know that
\mathcal{A}^{c}_{t}\cap\Phi_{t,n}(\mathcal{A}_{t}^{2c})
is open in the connected set
\mathcal{A}^{c}_{t}
. Let
(y_{n},w_{n})_{n\geq 0}
be a sequence in
\mathcal{A}^{c}_{t}\cap\Phi_{t,n}(\mathcal{A}_{t}^{2c})
converging to
(y,w)\in\mathcal{A}_{t}^{c}
. Let
(z_{n},v_{n})\in\mathcal{A}^{2c}_{t}
be such that
\Phi_{t,n}(z_{n},v_{n})=(y_{n},w_{n})
. By (
46
), we have

\forall\,n\in\mathbb{N},\qquad|(y_{n}-z_{n},w_{n}-v_{n})|=\big|\big(\mathcal{X}_{t}^{n},\mathcal{V}_{t}^{n}\big)\big|(z_{n},w_{n})\leq\frac{1}{2}c,

so that
(z_{n},v_{n})\in\mathcal{A}^{\frac{3}{2}c}_{t}
and
(z_{n},v_{n})_{n\geq 0}
is bounded. Consequently, there exists a subsequence that converges to
(z,v)\in\mathcal{A}^{2c}_{t}
, from which we get that
\mathcal{A}^{c}_{t}\cap\Phi_{t,n}(\mathcal{A}_{t}^{2c})
is closed in
