\mathcal{A}^{c}_{t}
.

We then deduce the result with
\mathcal{U}_{t,n}:=\Phi_{t,n}^{-1}\big(\mathcal{A}^{c}_{t}\cap\Phi_{t,n}(\mathcal{A}^{2c}_{t})\big)
. By (
46
), we have
\mathcal{U}_{t,n}\subset\mathcal{A}^{2c}_{t}
.
∎

Remark 8.2.1
.

In order to integrate by parts over
\{|x|\leq c_{n}t\}
, we will require
\Phi^{-1}_{t,n}-\mathrm{id}
to be bounded on
\{|x|\leq c_{n}t\}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
. Since the statement in Lemma
8.2.1
holds in fact for any
0<c^{\prime}_{n}\leq c_{n}
, we can assume it is indeed the case by considering a smaller
c_{n}
if necessary.

Thus, for
(t,x,v)\in[T_{n},\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
in the domain of invertibility, we can write

f(t,x,v)=g_{n}\big(t,\Xi_{t}^{-1}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{t,n}(x-tv,v)\big).

For our purposes, we need to derive an expansion for
\Xi_{t}^{-1}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{t,n}
and its derivatives. We begin with the next intermediary result.

Lemma 8.2.2
.

Let
t\geq T_{n}
and
(\mathfrak{Z}_{t}^{n},\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{n})
be the spatial and velocity components of
\Phi_{t,n}^{-1}-\mathrm{id}
. For any
\kappa=(\kappa_{x},\kappa_{v})
such that
|\kappa|\leq N-1-r_{n}
, there holds

\displaystyle\bigg|t^{|\kappa_{x}|}\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}\mathfrak{Z}_{t}^{n}(z,v)-\sum_{1\leq q\leq N+n-2-r_{n}-|\kappa|}\,\sum_{|\alpha|+p\leq q}\frac{z^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\mathbf{Z}^{\kappa}_{\alpha,q,p}(v)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\langle z,\log(t)\rangle^{N+n-1-r_{n}-|\kappa|}}{t^{N+n-1-r_{n}-|\kappa|}},

\displaystyle\bigg|t^{|\kappa_{x}|}\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{n}(z,v)-\sum_{q\leq N+n-2-r_{n}-|\kappa|}\,\sum_{|\alpha|+p\leq q}\frac{z^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q+1}}\mathbf{W}^{\kappa}_{\alpha,q,p}(v)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\langle z,\log(t)\rangle^{N+n-1-r_{n}-|\kappa|}}{t^{N+n-r_{n}-|\kappa|}},

for all
|z|<c_{n}t
, and
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
. Here,
\mathbf{Z}^{\kappa}_{\alpha,q,p},\,\mathbf{W}^{\kappa}_{\alpha,q,p}\in C^{0}\cap L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
.

Proof.

Let
|z|<c_{n}t
,
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, and
(y,w)=\Phi^{-1}_{t,n}(z,v)
, so that

z=y+\mathcal{X}_{t}^{n}(y,w),\quad v=w+\mathcal{V}_{t}^{n}(y,w),

and

y=z+\mathfrak{Z}_{t}^{n}(z,v),\quad w=v+\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{n}(z,v).

We then get from (
45
) that

|z-y|\lesssim|y|t^{-1}+t^{-1}\log(t),\qquad|v-w|\lesssim t^{-1}.

This allows us to show, according to the mean value theorem and
|y|<2c_{n}t
, that

\big|z-\mathcal{X}_{t}^{2}(z,v)-y\big|\lesssim\langle z,\log(t)\rangle^{2}\,t^{-2},\qquad\big|v-\mu t^{-1}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)-w\big|\lesssim\langle z,\log(t)\rangle\,t^{-2}.

Recall that
\mathbb{X}_{q,\alpha,p}
and
\mathbb{V}_{q,\alpha,p}
are of class
C^{N-r_{q+1}}
for any
1\leq q\leq n-1
. Then, iterating the above by using Taylor’s theorem instead of the mean value theorem, we obtain the stated expansions for
\mathfrak{Z}_{t}^{n}
and
\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{n}
. The cases with derivatives are handled as in the proof of Lemma
7.2.2
. The key identities are

\big[{\mathrm{d}}\Phi_{t,n}\big]^{-1}=\mathrm{id}+\sum_{k\geq 1}\,(-1)^{k}\big[{\mathrm{d}}\big(\mathcal{X}_{t}^{n},\mathcal{V}_{t}^{n}\big)\big]^{k}\quad\text{in $C^{N-1-r_{n}}\Big(\{|x|<c_{n}t\}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},~\mathfrak{M}_{6}({\mathbb{R}})\Big)$},

where
[{\mathrm{d}}(\mathcal{X}_{t}^{n},\mathcal{V}_{t}^{n})]
is the Jacobian matrix of
(\mathcal{X}_{t}^{n},\mathcal{V}_{t}^{n})=\Phi_{t,n}-\mathrm{id}
. We also use that

{\mathrm{d}}\Phi_{t,n}^{-1}(z,v)=\big[{\mathrm{d}}\Phi_{t,n}\big]^{-1}\big(\Phi_{t,n}^{-1}(z,v)\big).

The existence of the expansions for derivatives of
(\mathfrak{Z}_{t}^{n},\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{n})
ensues from an induction, Leibniz rule, Taylor’s theorem, and the expansions for
(\mathfrak{Z}_{t}^{n},\mathfrak{W}_{t}^{n})
.
∎

Using
\Xi_{t}^{-1}(x,v)=(x-\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v),v)
, the chain rule, and Taylor’s theorem, the previous Lemma
8.2.1
allows us to deduce the next corollary.

Corollary 8.2.3
.

Let
t\geq T_{n}
and
(\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n},\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n})
be the spatial and velocity components of
\Xi_{t}^{-1}\circ\Phi_{t,n}^{-1}-\mathrm{id}
. For any
\kappa=(\kappa_{x},\kappa_{v})
such that
|\kappa|\leq N-1-r_{n}
, there holds

\displaystyle\bigg|t^{|\kappa_{x}|}\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(z,v)+\delta_{0,|\kappa_{x}|}

\displaystyle\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}\phi_{\infty}(v)-\sum_{1\leq q\leq N+n-2-r_{n}-|\kappa|}\,\sum_{|\alpha|+p\leq q}\frac{z^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\mathbf{R}^{\kappa}_{\alpha,q,p}(v)\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lesssim\frac{\langle z,\log(t)\rangle^{N+n-1-r_{n}-|\kappa|}}{t^{N+n-1-r_{n}-|\kappa|}},

and

\displaystyle\bigg|t^{|\kappa_{x}|}\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(z,v)-\sum_{q\leq N+n-2-r_{n}-|\kappa|}\,\sum_{|\alpha|+p\leq q}\frac{z^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q+1}}\mathbf{S}^{\kappa}_{\alpha,q,p}(v)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\langle z,\log(t)\rangle^{N+n-1-r_{n}-|\kappa|}}{t^{N+n-r_{n}-|\kappa|}},

for all
|z|<c_{n}t
and
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
. Here,
\mathbf{R}^{\kappa}_{\alpha,q,p},\,\mathbf{S}^{\kappa}_{\alpha,q,p}\in C^{0}\cap L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
.

Remark 8.2.2
.

Note also that
N-1-r_{n}\geq n
and Lemma
2.6.3
gives

\forall\,q\in\llbracket 1,n-1\rrbracket,\qquad\mathbf{R}^{0}_{\alpha,q,p}\in C^{n}\cap W^{n,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}).

More generally, if
|\kappa|\leq N-r_{n+1}
and
q\in\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket
, we have
\mathbf{R}^{\kappa}_{\alpha,q,p},\,\mathbf{S}^{\kappa}_{\alpha,q-1,p}\in C^{n+1-q}\cap W^{n+1-q,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
. These properties will be used in order to apply Taylor’s theorem below.

8.2.2.
Step 2: The high order change of variables

We now know that if
t\geq T_{n}
and
|x-tv|<c_{n}t
, then

(47)

f(t,x,v)=g_{n}\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big),

and we have determined the asymptotic behaviour of
(\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n},\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n})
. So, we wish to perform the change of variables
y={\mathbf{Y}}_{t,z}^{n}(v)
, where the map
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n}\colon\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
defined on

\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}}:=\big\{v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}\;:\;|x-tv|<c_{n}t\big\}

is given by

{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n}(v):=x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v).

This change of variables would allow us to obtain an expansion in terms of the spatial averages of
g_{n}
.

The analysis of
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n}
can be reduced to the study of

\mathfrak{Z}_{t,x}(v):=v-t^{-1}\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),\quad\qquad\det{\mathrm{d}}{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n}(v)=-t^{3}\det{\mathrm{d}}\mathfrak{Z}_{t,x}(v).

Note that

(48)

{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n}(v)=y\qquad\textrm{if and only if}\qquad\mathfrak{Z}_{t,x}(v)=\frac{x-y}{t}.

Lemma 8.2.4
.

Let
t\geq T_{n}
and
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
. Then,
\mathfrak{Z}_{t,x}
is a
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism from
\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}}
to
\mathfrak{Z}_{t,x}(\mathcal{D}^{c_{n}}_{t,x})
. Moreover, for all
v\in\mathfrak{Z}_{t,x}(\mathcal{D}^{c_{n}}_{t,x})
, we have

\bigg|\mathfrak{Z}^{-1}_{t,x}(v)-v+\mu\frac{\log(t)}{t}\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)-\sum_{2\leq q\leq n}\;\sum_{|\alpha|+p\leq q}\frac{(x-tv)^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\Psi_{q,\alpha,p}(v)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\langle x-tv,\log(t)\rangle^{n+1}}{t^{n+1}},

where
\Psi_{q,\alpha,p}\in C^{n+2-q}\cap W^{n+2-q,\infty}\big(\mathfrak{Z}_{t,x}(\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}})\big)
.

Proof.

Note from Corollary
8.2.3
that, for any
|\kappa_{v}|\leq 1
, we have

(49)

\forall\,v\in\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}},\qquad t^{-1}\big|\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}\big[\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big]\big|\lesssim t^{-1}\log(t).

Consequently, if
T_{n}
is chosen large enough, we get as in the proof of Lemma
7.2.1
that

•

\mathfrak{Z}_{t,x}
is injective on
\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}}
,

•

\mathfrak{Z}_{t,x}
is a local
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism on
\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}}
,

These properties imply the first part of the result.

We now focus on the expansion, so we consider
w\in\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}}
and
v=\mathfrak{Z}_{t,x}(w)
. By definition
v=w-t^{-1}\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tw,w)
, so that
|v-w|\lesssim t^{-1}\log(t)
. By an induction, Taylor’s theorem, and the expansion for
\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}
given by Corollary
8.2.3
, we obtain the stated expansion for
\mathfrak{Z}^{-1}_{t,x}
. We have used Remark
8.2.2
in order to apply Taylor’s theorem at the required orders.

Finally, the regularity of
\Psi_{q,\alpha,p}
is obtained by Remark
8.2.2
. Indeed, one can check that
\Psi_{q,\alpha,p}
is a linear combination of product of quantities of the form
\partial_{v}^{\xi}\mathbf{R}^{0}_{q^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},p^{\prime}}
, where
q^{\prime}\geq 1
and
|\xi|+q^{\prime}\leq q-1
, or
\partial_{v}^{\kappa}\phi_{\infty}
with
1\leq|\kappa|\leq q
.
∎

We are now able to derive expansions for the two quantities related to
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n,-1}
that we are interested in.

Corollary 8.2.5
.

Let
t\geq T_{n}
and
x\!\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
. The map
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n}\!:v\mapsto x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)
is a
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism from
\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}}
to
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n}(\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}})
, which verifies

(50)

\Big\{y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\,:\,|y|<\frac{1}{2}c_{n}t\Big\}\subset{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n}(\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}})\subset\Big\{y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\,:\,|y|<2c_{n}t\Big\}.

Moreover, the inverse
{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}
satisfies that for all
y\in{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n}(\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}})
,

\bigg|{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y)-\frac{x}{t}-\sum_{1\leq q\leq n}\,\sum_{|\alpha|+p\leq q}\frac{y^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\mathbf{D}_{q,\alpha,p}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\langle y,\log(t)\rangle^{n+1}}{t^{n+1}},

where
\mathbf{D}_{q,\alpha,p}\in C^{0}\cap L^{\infty}(\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}})
, and

\bigg|{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y)+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}\big(x-t{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y),{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y)\big)-\frac{x}{t}-\sum_{1\leq q\leq n}\,\sum_{|\alpha|+p\leq q}\frac{y^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\overline{\mathbf{D}}_{q,\alpha,p}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\langle y,\log(t)\rangle^{n+1}}{t^{n+1}},

where
\overline{\mathbf{D}}_{q,\alpha,p}\in C^{0}\cap L^{\infty}(\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}})
. Moreover, the Jacobian determinant satisfies

\bigg|t^{3}\big|\det\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n,-1}\big|(y)-1-\sum_{1\leq q\leq n}\,\sum_{|\alpha|+p\leq q}\frac{y^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\mathbf{G}_{q,\alpha,p}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\langle y,\log(t)\rangle^{n+1}}{t^{n+1}},

where
\mathbf{G}_{q,\alpha,p}\in C^{0}\cap L^{\infty}(\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}})
.

Proof.

For the first part of the statement, we use (
48
) and the previous Lemma
8.2.4
. The inclusions (
50
) are implied by (
49
). For the first expansion, we exploit the one satisfied by
\mathfrak{Z}_{t,x}^{-1}
in Lemma
8.2.4
and we use (
48
) as well as Taylor’s theorem. For the second one, we use additionally the expansion verified by
\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}
in Corollary
8.2.3
and
N-1-r_{n}\geq n
. Finally, note that (
49
) implies

\big[{\mathrm{d}}\mathfrak{Z}_{t,x}\big]^{-1}(v)=\mathrm{id}+\sum_{k\geq 1}t^{-k}\big[{\mathrm{d}}\big(\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big)\big]^{k},

where
[{\mathrm{d}}(\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v))]_{ij}=-t\partial_{x^{j}}\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n,i}(x-tv,v)+\partial_{v^{j}}\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n,i}(x-tv,v)
. We then obtain the expansion for the Jacobian determinant using the multi-linearity of the determinant, Corollary
8.2.3
, Taylor’s theorem and

t^{3}\det{\mathrm{d}}{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n,-1}(y)=-\det\big[{\mathrm{d}}\mathfrak{Z}_{t,x}\big]^{-1}\big({\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n,-1}(y)\big).

∎

8.2.3.
Step 3: Expansion of
t^{3}\rho(G^{\beta}f)
in terms of the modified spatial averages

In order to lighten the presentation, we introduce the following terminology.

Definition 8.2.6
.

Let
h_{1},\;h_{2}:[T_{n},\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
with
d\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
. We say that
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
are
n
–equivalent
if there exists
C>0
such that

(51)

\forall\,(t,x)\in[T_{n},\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}_{x}^{3},\qquad|h_{1}-h_{2}|(t,x)\leq C\frac{\log^{S_{n+1}}(t)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}\,t^{n-2}}.

In the rest of this subsection
\mathbf{K}
will always denote a general function belonging to
C^{0}\cap L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
, which may be different from line to line.

Our goal consists in proving the next result by exploiting the previous two steps.

Proposition 8.2.7
.

Let
(t,x)\in[T_{n},\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
and
|\beta|\leq N-r_{n+1}
. Then,
t^{3}\rho(G^{\beta}f)
is
n
–equivalent to a linear combination of terms of the form

(52)

\frac{\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\mathbf{K}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\!\int_{|z|<t}z^{\alpha}\big[\partial_{v}^{\gamma_{v}}g_{n}\big]\Big(t,z,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}z,

with
|\gamma_{v}|\leq N-1-r_{n}
, and
p+|\alpha|\leq q\leq n
.

A preparatory result is required to treat the cases where
|\beta|\geq 1
.

Lemma 8.2.8
.

Let
|\beta|\leq N-r_{n}
and
(t,x,v)\in[T_{n},\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
be such that
|x-tv|<c_{n}t
. Then,
G^{\beta}f(t,x,v)
can be written as a linear combination of terms of the form

\prod_{1\leq i\leq p_{x}}\!\big[\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{i}}\mathfrak{X}^{n,k_{i}}_{t}\big](x-tv,v)\!\prod_{1\leq j\leq p_{v}}\!\big[\partial_{v}^{\gamma_{j}}\mathfrak{V}^{n,\ell_{j}}_{t}\big](x-tv,v)\big[\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}g_{n}\big]\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big),

where
k_{i},\,\ell_{j}\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket
,
\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n,k_{i}}
and
\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n,\ell_{j}}
are the
k_{i}
and
\ell_{j}
cartesian components of
\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}
and
\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}
,

|\alpha_{x}|+|\alpha_{v}|\leq|\beta|,\qquad 0\leq p_{x}=|\alpha_{x}|,\qquad 0\leq p_{v}\leq|\alpha_{v}|,\qquad\sum_{1\leq i\leq p_{x}}\sum_{1\leq j\leq p_{v}}|\kappa_{i}|+|\gamma_{j}|\leq|\beta|.

Remark 8.2.3
.

Recall from Section
7.2
that if
n=1
, then
(\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{1},\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{1})=(\mathfrak{X}_{t},\mathfrak{V}_{t})
does not depend on the spatial variable. So, in this case the result holds for all
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
.

Proof.

Let
h:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a distribution function. Using, the relations

\displaystyle\partial_{v^{i}}\big[h\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big)\big]=-\big[t\partial_{x^{i}}h\big]\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big[\partial_{v^{i}}h\big]\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big)

\displaystyle+\,\partial_{v^{i}}\big[\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big]\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}h\big]\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big)

\displaystyle+\,\partial_{v^{i}}\big[\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big]\cdot\big[\nabla_{v}h\big]\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big)

and

\partial_{v^{i}}[\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)]=-t[\partial_{x^{i}}\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}](x-tv,v)+[\partial_{v^{i}}\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}](x-tv,v),

we obtain in view of
G=t\nabla_{x}+\nabla_{v}
the identity

\displaystyle G_{i}\big[h\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big)\big]=\big[\partial_{v^{i}}h\big]\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big[\partial_{v^{i}}\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}\big](x-tv,v)\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}h\big]\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big)

\displaystyle+\,\big[\partial_{v^{i}}\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}\big](x-tv,v)\cdot\big[\nabla_{v}h\big]\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big)

.

The result is proven by iterating this relation and recalling (
47
).
∎

We now exploit the first step.

Proposition 8.2.9
.

Let
(t,x)\in[T_{n},\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
and
|\beta|\leq N-r_{n+1}
. Then,
t^{3}\rho(G^{\beta}f)
is
n
–equivalent to a linear combination of terms

\frac{\log^{p_{1}+p_{2}}(t)}{t^{q-3}}\int_{|x-tv|<c_{n}t}\mathbf{L}(v)\big(x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big)^{\alpha}\big[\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g_{n}\big]\!\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big){\mathrm{d}}v,

with
|\kappa_{x}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N-r_{n+1}
,
p_{1}+|\alpha|\leq q\leq n
,
p_{2}\leq|\kappa_{x}|
and
\mathbf{L}\in C^{n+1-q}\cap W^{n+1-q,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
.

Proof.

We remark first that by support considerations, Proposition
5.4.1
, and Remark
5.6.1
, we have

\displaystyle t^{3}\bigg|\rho(G^{\beta}f)(t,x)-\int_{|x-tv|<c_{n}t}G^{\beta}f(t,x,v){\mathrm{d}}v\bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\!\int_{|x-tv|\geq c_{n}t}\frac{t^{3}\,{\mathrm{d}}v}{t^{n+2}\langle x-tv\rangle^{7}\langle v\rangle^{7}}\sup_{(y,w)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\langle y-tw\rangle^{n+9}\langle w\rangle^{7}\big|G^{\beta}f\big|(t,y,w)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\frac{\log^{n+9+|\beta|}(t)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}\,t^{n-1}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}\,t^{n-2}}.

Since
N+n-2-r_{n}-N+r_{n+1}=2n-1\geq n
, the previous Lemma
8.2.8
together with the expansions in Corollary
8.2.3
and Remark
8.2.2
imply that

t^{3}\int_{|x-tv|<c_{n}t}G^{\beta}f(t,x,v){\mathrm{d}}v

can be written as a linear combination of terms of the form

(53)

\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\square\qquad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{t^{3}\log^{p_{1}^{\prime}+p_{2}^{\prime}}(t)}{t^{q^{\prime}}}\int_{|x-tv|<c_{n}t}\mathbf{L}(v)(x-tv)^{\alpha^{\prime}}\big[\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g_{n}\big]\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big){\mathrm{d}}v,

with
|\kappa_{x}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N-r_{n+1}
,
p_{1}^{\prime}+|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq q^{\prime}\leq n
,
p_{2}^{\prime}\leq|\kappa_{x}|
and
\mathbf{L}\in C^{n+1-q^{\prime}}\cap W^{n+1-q^{\prime},\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
, and terms bounded by

\mathfrak{Q}^{\kappa_{x},\kappa_{v}}_{t,x}:=\log^{|\kappa_{x}|}(t)t^{3}\int_{|x-tv|<c_{n}t}\frac{\langle x-tv,\log(t)\rangle^{n+1}}{t^{n+1}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g_{n}\big|\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big){\mathrm{d}}v,

with
|\kappa_{x}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq N-r_{n+1}
.

As
|\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(z,w)|\lesssim\log(t)
and
|\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(z,w)\lesssim 1
, we get by (
43
) and
N_{x}\geq 2n+8
that

\langle x-tv\rangle^{n+8}\langle v\rangle^{7}\big|\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g_{n}\big|\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v),v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big)\lesssim\log^{n+8}(t).

By Remark
5.6.1
, we then have

(54)

|\mathfrak{Q}^{\kappa_{x},\kappa_{v}}_{t,x}|\lesssim\frac{\log^{N-r_{n+1}+n+8}(t)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}\,t^{n-2}}\lesssim\frac{\log^{S_{n+1}}(t)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}\,t^{n-2}}.

We now work with the terms of the form (
53
). If
|\alpha^{\prime}|=0
they are already of the expected form. Otherwise,
(x-tv)^{\alpha^{\prime}}
can be written as a linear combination of terms

\big(x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big)^{\alpha_{1}}\big[\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)\big]^{\alpha_{2}},

with
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}=\alpha^{\prime}
. Using the expansion for
\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}
in Corollary
8.2.3
and (
54
) (in order to bound the strongly decaying terms), we obtain that (
53
) is
n
–equivalent to a linear combination of terms

\frac{t^{3}\log^{p_{1}+p_{2}^{\prime}}(t)}{t^{q}}\int_{|x-tv|<c_{n}t}\mathbf{M}(v)\big(x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}\big)^{\alpha_{1}}(x-tv)^{\alpha_{0}}\big[\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g_{n}\big]\big(t,x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n},v+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}\big){\mathrm{d}}v,

with
p_{1}+|\alpha_{1}|+|\alpha_{0}|\leq q\leq n
,
|\alpha_{0}|<|\alpha^{\prime}|
and
\mathbf{M}\in C^{n+1-q}\cap W^{n+1-q,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
. Note that we have dropped the dependence in
(x-tv,v)
of
(\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n},\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n})
. One can iterate the process to obtain the result since
|\alpha_{0}|<|\alpha^{\prime}|
.
∎

Next, we perform the high order change of variables which generalises
y=x-tv
.

Proposition 8.2.10
.

Let
(t,x)\in[T_{n},\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
and
|\beta|\leq N-r_{n+1}
. Then,
t^{3}\rho(G^{\beta}f)
is
n
–equivalent to a linear combination of terms of the form

\frac{\log^{p_{1}+p_{2}}(t)}{t^{q}}\mathbf{K}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\int_{y\in{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n}(\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}})}y^{\alpha}\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\gamma_{v}}g_{n}\big]\Big(t,y,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y,

with
|\gamma_{x}|+|\gamma_{v}|\leq N-1-r_{n}
,
p_{1}+|\alpha|\leq q\leq n
, and
p_{2}\leq|\gamma_{x}|
.

Proof.

We fix
(t,x)\in[T_{n},\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
. Apply first Proposition
8.2.9
and perform the change of variables
y={\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n}(v)=x-tv+\mathfrak{X}_{t}^{n}(x-tv,v)
allowed by Corollary
8.2.5
. We then have that

t^{3}\rho(G^{\beta}f)
is
n
–equivalent to a linear combination of terms of the form

\displaystyle\frac{\log^{p_{1}^{\prime}+p_{2}}(t)}{t^{q^{\prime}}}\int_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}\big({\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y)\big)y^{\alpha^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\times\big[\partial_{x}^{\kappa_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa_{v}}g_{n}\big]\Big(t,y,{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y)+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}\big(x-t{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y),{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y)\big)\Big)t^{3}\big|\det{\mathrm{d}}{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n,-1}\big|(y){\mathrm{d}}y,

with
|\kappa_{x}|+|\kappa_{v}|\leq\!N-r_{n+1}
,
p_{1}^{\prime}+|\alpha^{\prime}|\leq q^{\prime}\leq n
,
p_{2}\leq|\kappa_{x}|
, and the domain of integration is
\mathcal{D}_{t,x}\!:={\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n}(\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}})
.

Recall now the expansions satisfied by
{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y)
and
{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y)+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}\big(x-t{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y),{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y)\big)
in Corollary
8.2.5
. By Taylor’s theorem applied at order
n-q^{\prime}
, we get that the last quantity is the sum of terms

\frac{\log^{p_{1}+p_{2}}(t)}{t^{q}}\mathbf{K}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\int_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{t,x}}y^{\alpha}\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\gamma_{v}}g_{n}\big]\Big(t,y,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y,

where
p_{1}+|\alpha|\leq q\leq n
and
|\gamma_{x}|+|\gamma_{v}|\leq N-r_{n+1}+q\leq N-1-r_{n}
, and also terms bounded by

\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq 1}\frac{\log^{p_{1}^{\prime}+p_{2}}(t)}{t^{q^{\prime}}}\int_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{t,x}}\frac{\langle y,\log(t)\rangle^{n-q^{\prime}+1}}{t^{n-q^{\prime}+1}}|y|^{\alpha^{\prime}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\xi_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\xi_{v}}g_{n}\big|\Big(t,y,\frac{x}{t}+z_{\tau}(y)\Big){\mathrm{d}}y,

where
|\xi_{x}|+|\xi_{v}|\leq N-r_{n}
and

z_{\tau}(y):=\tau\Big({\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y)+\mathfrak{V}_{t}^{n}\Big(x-t{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y),{\mathbf{Y}}^{n,-1}_{t,x}(y)\Big)-\frac{x}{t}\Big),\qquad y\in\mathcal{D}_{t,x},\;\tau\in[0,1].

To conclude the proof, it remains us to prove that for any
|\xi_{x}|+|\xi_{v}|\leq N-r_{n}
and all
\tau\in[0,1]
, we have

(55)

\int_{y\in\mathcal{D}_{t,x}}\langle y\rangle^{n-q^{\prime}+1+|\alpha^{\prime}|}\big|\partial_{x}^{\xi_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\xi_{v}}g_{n}\big|\Big(t,y,\frac{x}{t}+z_{\tau}(y)\Big){\mathrm{d}}y\lesssim\frac{1}{\langle x/t\rangle^{3}}\lesssim\frac{t^{3}}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}}.

Recall from Corollary
8.2.5
that
\mathcal{D}_{t,x}\subset\{|y|<2c_{n}t\}
. Hence, using
c_{n}<1
and the expansion for
z_{\tau}(y)
, still given by Corollary
8.2.5
, we get

\forall\,(\tau,y)\in[0,1]\times\mathcal{D}_{t,x},\qquad\big|z_{\tau}(y)\big|\lesssim c_{n}+t^{-1}\log(t)\leq 2.

We then obtain (
55
) by using

\langle y\rangle^{n-q^{\prime}+1+|\alpha^{\prime}|}\big|\partial_{x}^{\xi_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\xi_{v}}g_{n}\big|(t,y,v)\lesssim\langle y\rangle^{-4}\langle v\rangle^{-3},

which holds for all
t\geq 2
,
|y|\leq t
and
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
according to (
43
) and
N_{x}-n\geq n+5
.
∎

We are now ready to obtain Proposition
8.2.7
.

Proof of Proposition
8.2.7
.

The proposition is a direct consequence of the previous result as well as the strong spatial decay estimates satisfied by
g_{n}
. We remark first that (
43
) implies that for all
t\geq T_{n}

(56)

\forall\,\frac{1}{2}c_{n}t\leq|z|\leq t,\;\forall\,v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},\qquad\langle z\rangle^{|\alpha|}\langle v\rangle^{3}\big|\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\gamma_{v}}g_{n}\big|(t,z,v)\lesssim t^{-N_{x}+n+|\alpha|}\lesssim t^{-n-2+|\alpha|},

for any
|\gamma_{x}|+|\gamma_{v}|\leq N-1-r_{n}
, and
|\alpha|\leq n
. Moreover, we have
\{|y|<\frac{1}{2}c_{n}t\}\subset{\mathbf{Y}}_{t,x}^{n}(\mathcal{D}_{t,x}^{c_{n}})
according to Corollary
8.2.5
. By Proposition
8.2.10
and the estimate (
56
), the normalised spatial average
t^{3}\rho(G^{\beta}f)
is then
n
–equivalent to a linear combination of terms of the form

\frac{\log^{p_{1}+p_{2}}(t)}{t^{q}}\mathbf{K}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\int_{|y|<t}y^{\alpha}\big[\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\gamma_{v}}g_{n}\big]\Big(t,y,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y,

with
|\gamma_{x}|+|\gamma_{v}|\leq N-r_{n+1}
,
p_{1}+|\alpha|\leq q\leq n
, and
p_{2}\leq|\gamma_{x}|
. We conclude the proof by performing integration by parts and by bounding the strongly decaying terms (which includes the boundary terms) through (
56
).
∎

8.2.4.
Step 4: Exploiting the convergence of the modified spatial averages

We are finally able to conclude the analysis of
t^{3}\rho(G^{\beta}f)
.

Proposition 8.2.11
.

Let
|\beta|\leq N-r_{n+1}
. Then, for all
(t,x)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
, we have

\bigg|t^{3}\rho(G^{\beta}f)(t,x)-\sum_{p\leq q\leq n}\frac{\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\partial_{v}^{\beta}\mathbf{F}_{p,q}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\log^{S_{n}+1}(t)}{\langle t+|x|\rangle^{3}\,t^{n-2}},

where
\mathbf{F}_{p,q}\in C^{N-r_{q+1}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
, and
\|\langle v\rangle^{3}\partial_{v}^{\beta}\mathbf{F}_{p,q}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\infty
.

Proof.

In view of the spatial (respectively velocity) decay satisfied by
\rho(G^{\beta}f)
(respectively
\partial_{v}^{\beta}\mathbf{F}_{p,q}
), and the compactness of
[2,T_{n}]
, it suffices to establish the result for
t\geq T_{n}
. We remark further that the existence of the asymptotic self-similar polyhomogeneous expansion (recall Definition
2.6.1
) implies by Lemma
2.6.4
that

\big[t^{3}\rho(G^{\beta}f)\big]_{p,q}=\partial_{v}^{\beta}\big[t^{3}\rho(f)\big]_{p,q}\qquad\textrm{and}\qquad\mathbf{F}_{p,q}\in C^{N-r_{n+1}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}).

Then, as such expansions are unique, we get from the induction hypothesis at order
q\in\llbracket 0,n-1\rrbracket
that
\mathbf{F}_{p,q}\in C^{N-r_{q+1}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
.

We then fix
(t,x)\in[T_{n},\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
. We first apply Proposition
8.2.7
to reduce the analysis to the study of terms of the form

(57)

\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\square\qquad\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\mathbf{K}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\!\int_{|z|<t}z^{\alpha}\big[\partial_{v}^{\gamma_{v}}g_{n}\big]\Big(t,z,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}z,

with
|\gamma_{v}|\leq N-1-r_{n}
, and
p+|\alpha|\leq q\leq n
. We start by dealing with the terms for which
q\geq 1
. Note that
N_{x}-n\geq 4+n
, so (
43
) implies

\frac{\log(t)}{t}\bigg|\int_{|z|<t}z^{\alpha}\big[\partial_{v}^{\gamma_{v}}g_{n}\big]\Big(t,z,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}z-\int_{|z|<t}z^{\alpha}\big[\partial_{v}^{\gamma_{v}}f_{\infty}\big]\Big(z,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}z\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\log^{S_{n}+1}(t)}{\langle x/t\rangle^{3}\,t^{n+1}}.

Furthermore, we get from Corollary
7.1.6
that
f_{\infty}
enjoys strong spatial decay, so

\frac{\log(t)}{t}\bigg|\int_{|z|\geq t}z^{\alpha}\big[\partial_{v}^{\gamma_{v}}f_{\infty}\big]\Big(z,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}z\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\log(t)}{\langle x/t\rangle^{3}\,t^{1+N_{x}-5-|\alpha|}}\lesssim\frac{\log(t)}{\langle x/t\rangle^{3}\,t^{n+1}},

since
N_{x}\geq 2n+5
. It remains to treat the case
q=0
, for which we have
|\alpha|=0
. It then suffices to use the strong convergence estimate for the modified non-weighted spatial averages (
44
) provided by the induction hypothesis. Finally,
\|\langle v\rangle^{3}\partial_{v}^{\beta}\mathbf{F}_{p,q}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\infty
ensues from the velocity decay of
f_{\infty}
and its derivatives.
∎

8.3.
Late-time asymptotics for the force field

In this section we will prove that, for any
|\gamma|\leq N-r_{n+1}
the normalised force field
t^{2}\nabla_{x}G^{\gamma}\phi
admits a polyhomogeneous expansion of order
n
along the modified spatial characteristics
t\mapsto(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}(t,x,v)+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n}(t,x,v))
. For this, we start by proving the next result.

Proposition 8.3.1
.

Let
\Phi_{p,q}\in C^{N-r_{q+1}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
be defined as

(58)

\Delta_{v}\Phi_{p,q}=\mathbf{F}_{p,q},

for any
p\leq q\leq n
. Then, we have
\Phi_{p,q}\in W^{N-r_{q+1},\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
, and for any
|\gamma|\leq N-r_{n+1}

\forall\,(t,x)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x},\qquad\bigg|t^{2+|\gamma|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,x)-\sum_{p\leq q\leq n}\frac{\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\big[\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi_{p,q}\big]\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\log^{1+S_{n}}(t)}{t^{n+1}}.

Proof.

Let
|\gamma|\leq N-r_{n+1}
, and set

\psi(t,x):=\sum_{p\leq q\leq n}\frac{\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi_{p,q}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big).

We note that

\nabla_{x}\psi(t,x)=\sum_{p\leq q\leq n}\frac{\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q+1}}[\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi_{p,q}]\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big).

Then, as
t^{2}\Delta_{x}[\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi_{p,q}(\frac{x}{t})]=[\Delta_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi_{p,q}](\frac{x}{t})
, we have

t^{2}\Delta_{x}\big(t^{1+|\gamma|}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi-\psi\big)(t,x)=t^{3}\rho\big(G^{\gamma}f\big)(t,x)-\sum_{p\leq q\leq n}\frac{\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\mathbf{F}_{p,q}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big),

so that

t^{2}\big|\Delta_{x}\big(t^{1+|\gamma|}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi-\psi\big)\big|(t,x)\lesssim\langle t+|x|\rangle^{-3}\,t^{-n+2}\log^{1+S_{n}}(t).

It remains to estimate
\nabla_{x}(t^{1+|\gamma|}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi-\psi)
by using (
15
). The boundedness of
\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi_{p,q}
is given by the estimates of
\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\mathbf{F}_{p,q}
in Proposition
8.2.11
, and Lemma
5.2.1
.
∎

We next show that the force field satisfies an asymptotic polyhomogeneous expansion in the sense of Definition
2.6.2
.

Proposition 8.3.2
.

For any
|\gamma|\leq N-r_{n+1}
, there exists
\overline{\Phi}_{q,\alpha,p}^{\,\gamma}\in C^{N-r_{q+1}}\cap W^{N-r_{q+1},\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
such that

\bigg|t^{2}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n}\big)-\sum_{q\leq n}\,\sum_{p+|\alpha|\leq q}\frac{x^{\alpha}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{q}}\overline{\Phi}^{\,\gamma}_{q,\alpha,p}(v)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\langle x\rangle^{n+1}\log^{1+S_{n}}(t)}{t^{n+1}},

for all
t\geq 2
, all
|x|\leq t
, and all
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
.

Proof.

Fix now
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
such that
|x|\leq t
and apply a
n^{\mathrm{th}}
-order Taylor expansion to get

\bigg|t^{2}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n}\big)-t^{2}\sum_{|\alpha|\leq n}\frac{\big({\mathbf{X}}_{n}+t({\mathbf{V}}_{n}-v)\big)^{\alpha}}{\alpha!}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi(t,tv)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\epsilon\,\langle{\mathbf{X}}_{n}+t({\mathbf{V}}_{n}-v)\rangle^{n+1}}{t^{n+1+|\gamma|}}.

The RHS can be bounded for
t\geq|x|
, by using
|{\mathbf{X}}_{n}-x|\lesssim\log(t)
and
t|{\mathbf{V}}_{n}-v|\lesssim 1
. Then, to derive the result:

(1)

We use the expansion obtained for
\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi
in Proposition
8.3.1
. If
|\alpha|\geq 1
, we appeal to the induction hypothesis at order
n-|\alpha|
in order to get the corresponding expansion for
\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi
. Note for this that
|\alpha|+|\gamma|\leq N-r_{n-|\alpha|+1}
.

(2)

We expand
({\mathbf{X}}_{n}+t({\mathbf{V}}_{n}-v))^{\alpha}
.

(3)

Finally, some terms thus obtained are lower order, so they have to be included in the error term. For this, we use that
\mathbb{X}_{q,\alpha,p},\,\mathbb{V}_{q,\alpha,p},\,\nabla_{v}\Phi_{p,q}\in W^{N-r_{q+1},\infty}_{v}
, and that
|x|\leq t
.

∎

Remark 8.3.1
.

One can check that
\overline{\Phi}^{\,\gamma}_{1,\alpha,0}=\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\alpha}\phi_{\infty}
if
|\gamma|=0
and
|\alpha|=1
. Since
{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}
will verify (
61
) below, we will have that
\mathbb{X}_{1,\alpha,0}=-\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\alpha}\phi_{\infty}
.

8.4.
Improved modified scattering

Let us begin by assuming that we have constructed
{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}
. Then, by the same computations as in (
29
), we have

(59)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g_{n+1}(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=\big(\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}-t\mu\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})\big)\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1},{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\mu\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})+\partial_{t}{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\big)\cdot\big[Gf\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1},{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\big).

Now, in view of the required form of
{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}
and
{\mathbf{X}}_{n}
, we have

(60)

\forall\,(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},\qquad|{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}-{\mathbf{X}}_{n}|+t|{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}-{\mathbf{V}}_{n}|\lesssim\frac{\langle x\rangle^{n}\log^{n}(t)}{t^{n}}.

Thus, we expect the difference

\nabla_{x}\phi\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\big)-\nabla_{x}\phi\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n}\big)

to be strongly decaying.

We then define
({\mathbf{X}}_{n+1},{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})
as the
unique polyhomogeneous map
such that, for all
t\geq 2
,
|x|\leq t
and
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

(61)

\displaystyle\big|t\mu\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}(t,x,v)+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n}(t,x,v)\big)-\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}(t,x,v)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\langle x\rangle^{n+1}\log^{1+S_{n}}(t)}{t^{n+2}},

(62)

\displaystyle\big|\mu\nabla_{x}\phi\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}(t,x,v)+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n}(t,x,v)\big)+\partial_{t}{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}(t,x,v)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\langle x\rangle^{n+1}\log^{1+S_{n}}(t)}{t^{n+3}}

and
({\mathbf{X}}_{n+1},{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})
has the constant in time term
(x,v)
. Recall Lemma
2.6.3
for the uniqueness of the expansions. In view of Proposition
8.3.2
, the maps
{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}
are indeed of the form stated in (
41
)–(
42
).

The next step consists in computing the derivatives of
\partial_{t}g_{n+1}
. For convenience, we introduce a notation.

Definition 8.4.1
.

Let
a\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and
b\in\llbracket 1,N-r_{n+1}\rrbracket
. We denote by
P_{a,b}^{n+1}({\mathbf{X}})
any quantity of the form

\prod_{1\leq i\leq a}\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma_{i}}{\mathbf{X}}^{k_{i}}_{n+1},\qquad\quad k_{i}\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket,\quad\sum_{1\leq i\leq a}|\gamma_{i}|=b,\quad|\gamma_{i}|\geq 1.

We set
P_{0,b}^{n+1}({\mathbf{X}})=1
. And we define
P_{a,b}^{n+1}({\mathbf{V}})
similarly.

We can easily bound these quantities.

Lemma 8.4.2
.

Let
a\in\mathbb{N}
and
b\in\llbracket 0,N-r_{n+1}\rrbracket
. Then, for all
t\geq 2
,

\forall\,|x|\leq t,\quad\forall\,v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},\qquad\big|P_{a,b}^{n+1}({\mathbf{X}})\big|(t,x,v)\lesssim\epsilon^{a}\log^{a}(t)+1,\qquad\big|P_{a,b}^{n+1}({\mathbf{V}})\big|(t,x,v)\lesssim 1.

As expected, these terms do not generate strongly divergent factors in the error terms.

Lemma 8.4.3
.

Let
|\kappa|\leq N-r_{n+1}
and
(t,x,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
. Then,
\partial_{t}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}g_{n+1}(t,x,v)
can be written as a linear combination of terms of the first kind

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}^{1,\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,b,c}:=~

\displaystyle\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}\Big(\!\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}\!-t\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})\!\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G^{\beta}\!f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}\!+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1},{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\big)P_{|\alpha|,\,b}^{n+1}({\mathbf{X}})P_{|\beta|,\,c}^{n+1}({\mathbf{V}}),

or of the second kind

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}^{2,\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,b,c}:=~

\displaystyle\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}\Big(\!\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})-\partial_{t}{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\!\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\big[G\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G^{\beta}\!f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1},{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\big)P_{|\alpha|,\,b}^{n+1}({\mathbf{X}})P_{|\beta|,\,c}^{n+1}({\mathbf{V}}),

where
|\gamma|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq|\kappa|
, and
b+c\leq|\kappa|
.

Remark 8.4.1
.

If
\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}=\partial_{v}^{\kappa}
contains only velocity derivatives, then so does
\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}
.

Proof.

Recall that
G=t\nabla_{x}+\nabla_{v}
, so that with
\partial_{i}
denoting either
\partial_{x^{i}}
or
\partial_{v^{i}}
, we have

(63)

\partial_{i}\big[h(t,{\mathbf{X}}+t{\mathbf{V}},{\mathbf{V}})\big]=\partial_{i}{\mathbf{X}}\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}h\big](t,{\mathbf{X}}+t{\mathbf{V}},{\mathbf{V}})+\partial_{i}{\mathbf{V}}\cdot\big[Gh\big](t,{\mathbf{X}}+t{\mathbf{V}},{\mathbf{V}}),

for any
C^{1}
function
h:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}\to{\mathbb{R}}
, and maps
{\mathbf{X}},\,{\mathbf{V}}:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{*}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
. We then obtain the result from an induction, where the base case is treated in (
59
), and the induction step is treated by using the relation (
63
) for
({\mathbf{X}},{\mathbf{V}})=({\mathbf{X}}_{n+1},{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})
.
∎

Next, we control the first factor in these two types of error terms.

Lemma 8.4.4
.

For any
|\alpha_{x}|+|\alpha_{v}|\leq N-r_{n+1}
and for all
(t,x,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
with
t\geq|x|
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\Big(\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}-t\mu\nabla_{x}\phi\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\big)\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{n+1}\log^{1+S_{n}}(t)t^{-n-2},

\displaystyle\Big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\Big(\mu\nabla_{x}\phi\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\big)+\partial_{t}{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{n+1}\log^{1+S_{n}}(t)t^{-n-3}.

Proof.

According to Proposition
5.2.2
, the mean value theorem, and (
60
), we have

t^{|\gamma|}\big|\big[\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\big)-\big[\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\gamma}\phi\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n}\big)\big|\lesssim\epsilon t^{-3-n}\langle x,\log(t)\rangle^{n},

for any
|\gamma|\leq N-r_{n+1}
. In view of the definition of
({\mathbf{X}}_{n+1},{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})
, it implies that:

•

The two estimates in the statement hold for
|\alpha_{x}|+|\alpha_{v}|=0
.

•

\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{x}}\partial_{v}^{\alpha_{v}}\big[\nabla_{x}\phi\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\big)\big]
admits an asymptotic polyhomogeneous expansion of order
n+2+|\alpha_{x}|
(with an error term decaying at least as
\langle x\rangle^{n+1}\log^{1+S_{n}}(t)t^{-n-3-|\alpha_{x}|}
), by using the chain rule, and Proposition
8.3.2
. Thus, so do the LHS in the second estimate of the statement. The LHS in the first estimate admits an expansion of order
n+1+|\alpha_{x}|
because of the additional factor
t
.

These two properties together with Lemma
2.6.3
, imply the result. Note that because of the last part of Lemma
2.6.3
, the spatial derivatives do not provide an improved estimate.
∎

We are finally able to conclude this subsection. We recall that
S_{n+1}\geq S_{n}+N+1-r_{n+1}
.

Proposition 8.4.5
.

For any
|\kappa|\leq N-r_{n+1}
, there holds

\forall\,t\geq 2,\;\forall|x|\leq t,\;\forall v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},\qquad\langle x\rangle^{N_{x}-n-1}\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\big|\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}g_{n+1}(t,x,v)-\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}f_{\infty}(x,v)\big|\lesssim\frac{\log^{S_{n+1}}(t)}{t^{n+1}}.

Proof.

Since
|{\mathbf{X}}_{1}-{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}|\lesssim 1
and
|v-{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}|\lesssim t^{-1}
on the set
\{|x|\leq t\}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have by Lemma
7.1.4
that

(64)

\forall\,t\geq 2,\;\forall|x|\leq t,\;\forall v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},\qquad\langle x\rangle^{N_{x}}\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\big|\partial_{x}^{\gamma_{x}}G^{\gamma_{v}}f\big|\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1},{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\big)\lesssim\log^{|\gamma_{v}|}(t),

for any
|\gamma_{x}|+|\gamma_{v}|\leq N-1
. Combining this estimate with the Lemmata
8.4.2
–
8.4.3
–
8.4.4
, we have

\forall\,t\geq 2,\;\forall|x|\leq t,\;\forall v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},\qquad\langle x\rangle^{N_{x}-n-1}\,\langle v\rangle^{N_{v}}\big|\partial_{t}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}g_{n+1}\big|(t,x,v)\lesssim\frac{\log^{1+S_{n}+N-r_{n+1}}(t)}{t^{n+2}}.

To conclude the proof, it remains to show that
f_{\infty}
is indeed the limit of
g_{n+1}
. For this, we use that
f_{\infty}
is the limit of
g_{n}
, and that

|g_{n+1}(t,x,v)-g_{n}(t,x,v)|\leq\|\nabla_{x,v}g_{n}(t,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\big(|{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}-{\mathbf{X}}_{n}|+t|{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}-{\mathbf{V}}_{n}|),

according to the mean value theorem. By (
60
) and (
43
), the RHS converges in
L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
to zero.
∎

8.5.
Strong convergence estimates for the spatial averages of
g_{n+1}

A direct application of Proposition
8.4.5
provides that the spatial average of
g_{n+1}
converges to the one of
f_{\infty}
as
t^{-n-1}\log^{S_{n+1}}t
. As we performed in Section
7.1
for the analysis of
g_{1}
, we are in fact able to establish an enhanced convergence estimate for the spatial average of
g_{n+1}
. For this, we can decompose
\partial_{t}\partial_{x,v}^{\kappa}g_{n+1}
as in Lemma
8.4.3
into the sum of terms of the first and second kind. The terms of the first kind decay as
t^{-n-2}\log^{1+S_{n}}(t)
and they govern the rate of convergence in Proposition
8.4.5
. In contrast, the terms of the second kind decay as
t^{-n-3}\log^{1+S_{n}}(t)
.

In order to obtain the improved convergence estimate, we need to carefully study the spatial average of the terms of the first kind. The key idea will be to exploit that these terms carry a factor of the form
\nabla_{x}Z^{\xi}f
allowing for integration by parts.

Proposition 8.5.1
.

Let
|\kappa|\leq N-1-r_{n+1}
. Then, there exist
\mathbf{Q}_{p,\xi}^{\kappa,\beta}\in C^{0}\cap L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
such that

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{|z|<t}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}g_{n+1}(t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}\partial_{v}^{\kappa}f_{\infty}(z,v){\mathrm{d}}z-\sum_{|\beta|\leq|\kappa|}\sum_{p+|\xi|\leq n}

\displaystyle\frac{\log^{p}(t)}{t^{n+1}}\mathbf{Q}_{p,\xi}^{\kappa,\beta}(v)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}z^{\xi}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\infty}(z,v){\mathrm{d}}z\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-n-2}\log^{S_{n+1}}(t)

,

for all
(t,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
.

Proof.

Fix
(t,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
. We start by applying Lemma
8.4.3
. There exists
N^{\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,b,c}\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\displaystyle\langle v\rangle^{3}\bigg|\partial_{t}\!\int_{|z|<t}

\displaystyle\partial_{v}^{\kappa}g_{n+1}(t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z-\sum_{|\gamma|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq|\kappa|}\,\sum_{b+c\leq|\kappa|}N_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,b,c}^{\kappa}\int_{|z|<t}\mathbb{T}^{1,\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,b,c}(t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z\bigg|

(65)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\langle v\rangle^{3}\big|\partial_{v}^{\kappa}g_{n+1}(t,t\omega,v)\big|t^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}+\sup_{|\gamma|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq|\kappa|}\,\sup_{b+c\leq|\kappa|}\langle v\rangle^{3}\bigg|\int_{|z|<t}\mathbb{T}^{2,\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,b,c}(t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z\bigg|,

where
{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
is the volume form on the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{2}
. The goal consists in proving the following properties:

•

Any error term in the RHS decay faster than
t^{-n-3}\log^{S_{n+1}}(t)
.

•

The integral over
\{|z|\!<\!t\}
of any term
\mathbb{T}^{1,\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,b,c}(t,z,v)
is
(n\!+\!2)
–equivalent to a sum of terms

(66)

\frac{\log^{p}(t)}{t^{n+2}}\overline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\,p,\xi}^{\kappa,\beta}(v)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}z^{\xi}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\infty}(z,v){\mathrm{d}}z,\qquad p+|\xi|\leq n.

To conclude, it remains to take the antiderivatives in time of quantities of the form (
66
). Here, we identify the limit by applying Proposition
8.4.5
, which implies

\lim_{t\to+\infty}\int_{|z|<t}g_{n+1}(t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}f_{\infty}(z,v){\mathrm{d}}z.

Control of boundary terms.
Recall that
N_{x}\geq 2n+6
. We control the boundary term in (
65
) by applying Proposition
8.4.5
, together with Corollary
7.1.6
in order to estimate
f_{\infty}
. So, for every
|\xi|\leq N-r_{n+1}
we have

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\big|\partial_{v}^{\xi}g_{n+1}(t,t\omega,v)\big|{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\lesssim t^{-N_{x}+n+1}\langle v\rangle^{-N_{v}}\lesssim t^{-n-5}\langle v\rangle^{-3}.

We will deal with other boundary terms by the next consequence of Corollary
7.1.6
,

(67)

\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\square\qquad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{B}_{\gamma}(t,v):=\!\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\big|\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma}g_{1}\big|\big(t,t\omega,v\big){\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\omega}}\lesssim t^{-N_{x}+1}\langle v\rangle^{-3},

with
|\gamma|\leq N-2
.

Estimate for terms of the second kind.
Let us fix
b+c\leq|\kappa|
and
|\gamma|+|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq|\kappa|
. Combining (
64
) with the Lemmata
8.4.2
and
8.4.4
, we bound the terms of the second kind by

(68)

\forall\,|z|\leq t,\qquad\big|\mathbb{T}^{2,\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,b,c}\big|(t,z,v)\lesssim\langle z\rangle^{-4}\langle v\rangle^{-3}t^{-n-3}\log^{1+S_{n}+N-r_{n+1}}(t).

Consequently, these terms give rise to error terms decaying as
t^{-n-3}\log^{S_{n+1}}(t)
in (
65
).

Estimate for terms of the first kind.
We now focus on the term of the first kind
\mathbb{T}^{1,\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,b,c}(t,z,v)
, that is, terms of the form

\displaystyle\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Big(\!\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}\!-t\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})\!\Big)\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G^{\beta}\!f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}\!+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1},{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\big)P_{|\alpha|,\,b}^{n+1}({\mathbf{X}})P_{|\beta|,\,c}^{n+1}({\mathbf{V}}).

From now on, we study the decay properties of a fixed term
\mathbb{T}^{1,\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,b,c}
.

Reduction of the analysis to
\overline{\mathbb{T}}{\vphantom{\mathbb{T}}}^{\,1}
.
We first reduce the analysis to the study of

\overline{\mathbb{T}}{\vphantom{\mathbb{T}}}^{\,1}(t,z,v):=\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Big(\!\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}\!-t\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})\!\Big)\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G^{\beta}\!f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}\!+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1},{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}\big)P_{|\alpha|,\,b}^{1}({\mathbf{X}}),

where
P_{|\alpha|,\,b}^{1}({\mathbf{X}})
is obtained by formally replacing
{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}
by
{\mathbf{X}}_{1}
, so that

P_{|\alpha|,\,b}^{1}({\mathbf{X}}):=\log^{|\alpha|}(t)\prod_{1\leq i\leq|\alpha|}\nabla_{v}\partial_{x,v}^{\gamma_{i}}\phi_{\infty}^{k_{i}}(v),\qquad k_{i}\in\llbracket 1,3\rrbracket,\quad\sum_{1\leq i\leq|\alpha|}|\gamma_{i}|=b,\quad|\gamma_{i}|\geq 1.

For this, we note that:

•

By the form (
41
) of
{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}-{\mathbf{X}}_{1}
, we have
|P_{|\alpha|,\,b}^{n+1}({\mathbf{X}})-P_{|\alpha|,\,b}^{1}({\mathbf{X}})|\lesssim\langle z,\log(t)\rangle\,t^{-1}\log^{|\alpha|-1}(t)
.

•

By the form (
42
) of
{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}-v
, either
|P_{|\beta|,\,c}^{n+1}({\mathbf{V}})|\lesssim t^{-1}
or
|P_{|\beta|,\,c}^{n+1}({\mathbf{V}})-1|\lesssim t^{-1}
.

•

Thus, by (
64
) and the Lemmata
8.4.2
and
8.4.4
, one of the next two estimates hold:

\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\square\qquad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\big|\mathbb{T}^{1,\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,b,c}\big|(t,z,v)\lesssim\frac{\log^{S_{n}+N-r_{n+1}}(t)}{\langle z\rangle^{4}\langle v\rangle^{3}t^{n+3}},\qquad\quad\big|\mathbb{T}^{1,\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,b,c}-\overline{\mathbb{T}}{\vphantom{\mathbb{T}}}^{\,1}\big|(t,z,v)\lesssim\frac{\log^{S_{n}+N-r_{n+1}}(t)}{\langle z\rangle^{4}\langle v\rangle^{3}t^{n+3}}.

Therefore, if the first estimate holds, the term
\mathbb{T}^{1,\kappa}_{\gamma,\alpha,\beta,b,c}
is of lower order. Otherwise, if the second estimate holds, we carry on with the analysis of
\overline{\mathbb{T}}{\vphantom{\mathbb{T}}}^{\,1}
.

Reduction of the analysis to
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}{\vphantom{\mathbb{T}}}^{\,1}
.
We next show that we can in fact focus on

\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}{\vphantom{\mathbb{T}}}^{\,1}(t,z,v):=\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Big(\!\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}\!-t\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})\!\Big)\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G^{\beta}\!f\big]\big(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{1},{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big)P_{|\alpha|,\,b}^{1}({\mathbf{X}})

since the difference
\overline{\mathbb{T}}{\vphantom{\mathbb{T}}}^{\,1}-\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}{\vphantom{\mathbb{T}}}^{\,1}
is lower order, that is

\forall\,|z|\leq t,\qquad\big|\overline{\mathbb{T}}{\vphantom{\mathbb{T}}}^{\,1}-\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}{\vphantom{\mathbb{T}}}^{\,1}\big|(t,z,v)\lesssim\frac{\log^{1+S_{n}+N-r_{n+1}}(t)}{\langle z\rangle^{4}\langle v\rangle^{3}t^{n+3}}.

For this, we apply Lemma
8.4.4
, and we use the mean value theorem together with Lemma
7.1.4
as well as

\forall\,|z|\leq t,\qquad\big|{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}-{\mathbf{X}}_{1}\big|(t,z,v)+t\big|{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}-{\mathbf{V}}_{1}\big|(t,z,v)\lesssim\frac{1}{t}\langle z,\log(t)\rangle\,.

Comparison with
g_{1}
and conclusion.
Our last reduction consists in applying Remark
8.2.3
and Lemma
7.2.2
, in order to write
G^{\beta}f
in terms of the derivatives of
g_{1}
. This implies that
\widetilde{\mathbb{T}}{\vphantom{\mathbb{T}}}^{\,1}(t,z,v)
can be written as a linear combination of terms

•

either decaying as
\langle z\rangle^{-4}\langle v\rangle^{-3}t^{-n-3}\log^{1+S_{n}+N-r_{n+1}}(t)
,

•

or of the form

\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\square\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{T}(t,z,v):=\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Big(\!\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}\!-t\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})\!\Big)\cdot\big[\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\nu}\partial_{v}^{\mu}g_{1}\big](t,z,v)P_{|\nu|,\,b}^{1}({\mathbf{X}}),

where
|\nu|\geq|\alpha|
, and
|\nu|+|\mu|\leq|\alpha|+|\beta|
.

Note now that integration by parts yields, as
{\mathbf{X}}_{1}-x
and
{\mathbf{V}}_{1}
are independent of
x
, that

\displaystyle\langle v\rangle^{3}\bigg|\int_{|z|<t}

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\cdot\partial_{x}^{\nu}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Big(\!\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}\!-t\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})\!\Big)\big[\partial_{v}^{\mu}g_{1}\big](t,z,v)P_{|\nu|,\,b}^{1}({\mathbf{X}})-\mathbb{T}^{1}(t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\langle t\rangle^{n+1}\log^{1+S_{n}+|\nu|}(t)}{t^{n+2}}\sup_{|\xi|\leq|\nu|+|\mu|}\langle v\rangle^{3}\mathcal{B}_{\xi}(t,v)\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{n+3}},

where we used Lemma
8.4.4
, (
67
), and
N_{x}-1\geq n+3
in the last step. Assume first that
|\nu|+1\leq n
and note that
|\gamma|+|\nu|+1\leq N-r_{n+1}
. Then:

•

Recall from Lemma
8.4.4
that
\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\nu}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}(\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}\!-t\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}))
admits an asymptotic polyhomogeneous expansion of order
n+2+|\nu|
. Here, the error term decays as
\langle x\rangle^{n+1}\!\log^{1+S_{n}}(t)t^{-n-3-|\nu|}
.

•

According to Lemma
8.4.4
, all the terms of order up to
n+1
vanish.

Consequently, there exist
\mathbf{K}^{\xi}_{p}\in C^{0}\cap L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
such that, for all
|z|\leq t
and all
v\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\bigg|\nabla_{x}\cdot\partial_{x}^{\nu}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Big(\!\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}\!-t\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})\!\Big)-\!\sum_{p+|\xi|\leq n-|\nu|}\!\frac{z^{\xi}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{n+2}}\mathbf{K}^{\xi}_{p}(v)\bigg|\lesssim\!\frac{\langle z\rangle^{n+1}\log^{1+S_{n}}(t)}{t^{n+3}}.

Next, we use the particular form of
P^{1}_{|\nu|,b}({\mathbf{X}})
to show that there exists
\mathbf{L}^{\xi}_{p}\in C^{0}\cap L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
such that

\displaystyle\langle v\rangle^{3}\bigg|\int_{|z|<t}\mathbb{T}(t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z-\!\sum_{p+|\xi|\leq n}\!\frac{\log^{p}(t)}{t^{n+2}}\mathbf{L}_{p}^{\xi}(v)\int_{|z|<t}z^{\xi}\big[\partial_{v}^{\beta}g_{1}\big](t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\log^{1+S_{n}}(t)}{t^{n+3}}.

It remains to apply Corollary
7.1.6
for
|\xi|\leq n
, which provides

\bigg|\int_{|z|<t}z^{\xi}\big[\partial_{v}^{\beta}g_{1}\big](t,z,v){\mathrm{d}}z-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}z^{\xi}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\infty}(z,v){\mathrm{d}}z\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\log^{N}(t)}{t}\lesssim\frac{\log^{S_{n+1}-n}(t)}{t}.

If
|\nu|\geq n
, we use
|\partial_{x}^{\zeta}{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}|(t,z,v)+t|\partial_{x}^{\zeta}{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1}|(t,z,v)\lesssim t^{-|\zeta|}
and
|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\zeta}\phi|(t,x)\lesssim\epsilon t^{-2-|\zeta|}
to argue that

\big|\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\nu}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\big(\partial_{t}{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}\!-t\nabla_{x}\phi(t,{\mathbf{X}}_{n+1}+t{\mathbf{V}}_{n+1})\big)\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{n+3}}.

One can then deduce that
\mathbb{T}
also satisfies (
68
). This concludes the proof.
∎

9.
Non-linear tails and weak convergence

In this section, we obtain late-time tails for the spatial density and the force field. We also prove that the distribution (up to normalisation) converges weakly to a Dirac mass in the zero velocity set. We finally capture the shearing of the system with a weak convergence statement.

9.1.
Hierarchy of asymptotic conservation laws

Let
f_{\infty}\colon{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a regular scattering state. Let
|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N-2
be multi-indices. We consider the
weighted spatial averages

\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}_{\beta}:{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}\to{\mathbb{R}}
given by

\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}_{\beta}(v):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}x^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\alpha+\beta}f_{\infty}(x,v){\mathrm{d}}x.

By Proposition
4.2.2
, we note that
\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}_{\beta}
is bounded by a weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
norm of the scattering state
f_{\infty}
. Moreover, the weighted spatial averages
\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}_{\beta}
can be defined as

\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}_{\beta}(v)=\lim_{t\to\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}x^{\alpha}\partial_{v}^{\alpha+\beta}g_{1}(t,x,v){\mathrm{d}}x,

by Corollary
7.1.6
. See for comparison Proposition
4.2.1
for the definition of the conservation laws of the linearised system.

9.2.
Late-time tail for the spatial density

Let
f\colon[0,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a small regular solution for the Vlasov–Poisson system. The late-time asymptotics of the spatial density are obtained in Proposition
8.2.11
. In this subsection, we apply this result to obtain non-linear tails for the spatial density. We begin writing the tail of the self-similar profile
\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}_{\beta}(\frac{x}{t})
in terms of the conservation laws
\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}_{\beta}(0)
.

Lemma 9.2.1
.

Let
N\geq 2
and
|\alpha|+|\beta|+|\gamma|\leq N-2
. Then, the weighted spatial average
\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}_{\beta}
satisfies that for all
(t,x)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
,

\Big|\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}_{\beta}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)-\sum_{|\gamma|\leq N-|\alpha|-|\beta|}\frac{1}{\gamma!}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}_{\beta}(0)\frac{x^{\gamma}}{t^{|\gamma|}}\Big|\lesssim\frac{|x|^{N+1-|\alpha|-|\beta|}}{t^{N+1-|\alpha|-|\beta|}}

Proof.

The proof follows by a straightforward application of Taylor’s theorem (see the proof of Lemma
4.3.1
). Here, we have bounded the norm of the scattering state by the initial data norm.
∎

We similarly apply Taylor to obtain an expansion for the self-similar profile
\mathbf{F}_{p,q}(\frac{x}{t})
. Recall that by Proposition
8.2.11
, these profiles can be written in terms of the hierarchy of asymptotic conservation laws
\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}_{\beta}
. Moreover, according to Lemma
2.6.4
, we have
\mathbf{F}_{p,q}\in C^{N-r_{q+1}}\cap W^{N-r_{q+1},\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v})
.

Lemma 9.2.2
.

Let
N\geq 4
and
(p,q)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
such that
p\leq q
and
r_{q+1}\leq N
. For any integer
k\in\mathbb{N}
and multi-index
\beta
satisfying
k+|\beta|\leq N-r_{q+1}-1
, we have

\forall\,(t,x)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x},\qquad\bigg|\partial_{v}^{\beta}\mathbf{F}_{p,q}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)-\sum_{|\gamma|\leq k}\frac{1}{\gamma!}\partial_{v}^{\beta+\gamma}\mathbf{F}_{p,q}(0)\frac{x^{\gamma}}{t^{|\gamma|}}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{|x|^{k+1}}{t^{k+1}}.

Finally, we obtain late-time tails for the spatial density in terms of the hierarchy of asymptotic conservation laws
\mathcal{A}(f)
.

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
N\geq 4
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
r_{n+1}\leq N
. For any
|\beta|\leq N-r_{n+1}-1
and all
(t,x)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|t^{3}\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}G^{\beta}f(t,x,v){\mathrm{d}}v-\sum_{p\leq q\leq n}\,\sum_{|\gamma|\leq n-q}\frac{C_{\alpha}}{\gamma!}\partial_{v}^{\gamma+\beta}\mathbf{F}_{p,q}(0)\frac{x^{\gamma}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{|\gamma|+q}}\bigg|\lesssim\dfrac{\log^{S_{n}+1}(t)}{t^{n+1}}|x|^{n+1}.

Here,
\partial_{v}^{\gamma+\beta}\mathbf{F}_{p,q}(0)
can be computed in terms of
\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}_{\beta}(v)
and its derivatives for
|\alpha|+|\beta|\leq N-2
.

Proof.

Since
r_{n+1}=r_{n}+n+1
, the proof follows directly by Proposition
8.2.11
and Lemma
9.2.2
.
∎

9.3.
Late-time tail for the force field

The late-time asymptotics of the force field are obtained in Proposition
8.3.1
. In this subsection, we apply this result to obtain non-linear tails for the force field. We begin writing the tails of the self-similar profile
\nabla_{v}\Phi_{p,q}(\frac{x}{t})
in terms of the constants
\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma}\Phi_{p,q}(0)
.

Lemma 9.3.1
.

Let
N\geq 4
and
(p,q)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
such that
p\leq q
and
r_{q+1}\leq N
. For any integer
k\in\mathbb{N}
and multi-index
\beta
satisfying
k+|\beta|\leq N-r_{q+1}-1
, we have

\forall\,(t,x)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x},\qquad\bigg|\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\beta}\Phi_{p,q}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big)-\sum_{|\gamma|\leq k}\frac{1}{\gamma!}\nabla_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma+\beta}\Phi_{p,q}(0)\frac{x^{\gamma}}{t^{|\gamma|}}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{|x|^{k+1}}{t^{k+1}}.

Finally, we obtain late-time tails for the force field
\nabla_{x}\phi
in terms of the profiles
\Phi^{\beta}_{p,q}
. We recall that
\Phi^{\beta}_{p,q}
are explicitly defined using the hierarchy of asymptotic conservation laws
\mathcal{A}(f)
.

Theorem 9.2
.

Let
N\geq 4
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
r_{n+1}\leq N
. For any
|\beta|\leq N-r_{n+1}-1
and all
(t,x)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|t^{2+|\beta|}\nabla_{x}\partial_{x}^{\beta}\phi(t,x)-\sum_{p\leq q\leq n}\sum_{|\gamma|\leq n-q}\frac{C_{\alpha}}{\gamma!}\partial_{v}^{\gamma+\beta}\nabla_{v}\Phi_{p,q}(0)\frac{x^{\gamma}\log^{p}(t)}{t^{|\gamma|+q}}\bigg|\lesssim\dfrac{\log^{S_{n}+1}(t)}{t^{n+1}}|x|^{n+1}.

where
\Phi_{p,q}
is defined by
\Delta_{v}\partial_{v}^{\gamma+\beta}\Phi_{p,q}=\partial_{v}^{\gamma+\beta}\mathbf{F}_{p,q}
.

Proof.

The proof follows directly by Proposition
8.3.1
and Lemma
9.3.1
.
∎

9.4.
Weak convergence properties

In this section, we show the weak convergence of the normalised distribution
t^{3}G^{\beta}f
for small data solutions to the Vlasov–Poisson system in terms of the scattering state
\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\infty}
. For this purpose, we first compute the limit of the spatial averages studied in Proposition
6.1.1
in terms of the scattering state.

Proposition 9.4.1
.

Let
|\beta|\leq N-2
. For all
(t,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{x}^{3}}G^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{x}^{3}}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\infty}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\bigg|\lesssim\epsilon\frac{\log^{N-1}(t)}{t}.

In particular, the spatial average
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{x}^{3}}G^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\mathrm{d}x
converges to
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{x}^{3}}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\infty}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x
as
t\to\infty
.

Proof.

We recall that
[G^{\beta}f](t,x+tv,v)=\partial_{v}^{\beta}g_{0}(t,x,v)
, so it suffices to prove the result for
|\beta|=0
according to Proposition
6.1.1
. Then, by performing the change of variables
y(x)=x-tv-\mu\log(t)\nabla_{v}\phi_{\infty}(v)
, we observe that the spatial average of
f
and
g_{1}
are equal. It remains to apply Corollary
7.1.6
.
∎

In particular, one can show the following corollary.

Corollary 9.4.2
.

Let
|\beta|\leq N-3
. Then, for all
(t,v)\in[2,\infty)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, we have

\forall\,\bar{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v},\qquad\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{y}^{3}}G^{\beta}f\Big(t,y,\bar{v}+\frac{x}{t}\Big)\mathrm{d}y-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{y}^{3}}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\infty}(y,\bar{v})\mathrm{d}y\bigg|\lesssim\epsilon\langle x\rangle\frac{\log^{N-1}(t)}{t}.

Corollary
9.4.2
will be used in the forthcoming subsections to show the weak convergence statements of the distribution function.

9.4.1.
Concentration in the zero velocity set

In this section, we show that
t^{3}G^{\beta}f(t,x,v)
converges weakly to the Dirac mass
(\int\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\infty}(x,0)\mathrm{d}x)\delta_{v=0}(v)
. This the nonlinear version of Proposition
4.4.3
.

Proposition 9.4.3
.

Let
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{x,v}
be a compactly supported test function. Let
|\beta|\leq N-2
. Then, the Vlasov field
f
satisfies

\lim_{t\to\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}t^{3}G^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\varphi(x,v){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}v=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}\Big(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}(0)\delta_{v=0}(v)\Big)\varphi(x,v){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}v.

In other words, the distribution
t^{3}G^{\beta}f(t,x,v)
converges weakly to
\mathcal{A}_{\beta}(0)\delta_{v=0}(v)
as
t\to\infty
.

Proof.

Applying Lemma
4.4.2
, for fixed
t\geq 2
, to the distribution
g(x,v)=G^{\beta}f(t,x+vt,v)
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{x}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{v}^{3}}t^{3}G^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\varphi(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v~-

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{x}^{3}}\varphi\Big(x,\frac{x}{t}\Big)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}_{y}^{3}}G^{\beta}f\Big(t,y+x,\frac{x}{t}\Big)\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}x\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-1}\sup_{(x,v)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}\langle x\rangle^{5}\big(|G^{\beta}f|+|GG^{\beta}f|\big)\lesssim\log^{N-1}(t)t^{-1},

where the last estimate holds by Proposition
5.4.1
. Finally, we apply Fubini, the dominated convergence theorem, and Corollary
9.4.2
, to show

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\varphi\Big(x,\frac{x}{t}\Big)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{y}}G^{\beta}f\Big(t,y+x,\frac{x}{t}\Big){\mathrm{d}}y{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}}\varphi(x,0){\mathrm{d}}x\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{z}}\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\infty}(z,0){\mathrm{d}}z.

∎

9.4.2.
Shearing of the Hamiltonian flow

Let
\bar{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
. In this subsection, we show that
t^{3}G^{\beta}f(t,x+t\bar{v},v+\bar{v})
converges weakly to the Dirac mass
(\int\partial_{v}^{\beta}f_{\infty}(x,\bar{v})\mathrm{d}x)\delta_{v=\bar{v}}(v)
. This result is analogous to Proposition
4.4.5
in the nonlinear setting. The proof is similar to the one of Proposition
9.4.3
and relies on Corollary
9.4.2
.

Proposition 9.4.4
.

Let
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{x,v}
be a compactly supported test function. Let
\bar{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}
, and
|\beta|\leq N-2
. Then, the Vlasov field
f
satisfies

\lim_{t\to\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}t^{3}G^{\beta}f(t,x,v)\varphi(x-t\bar{v},v){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}v=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{x}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}_{v}}\Big(\mathcal{A}_{\beta}(\bar{v})\delta_{v=\bar{v}}(v)\Big)\varphi(x,v){\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}v.

In other words, the distribution
t^{3}G^{\beta}f(t,x+t\bar{v},v+\bar{v})
converges weakly to
\mathcal{A}_{\beta}(\bar{v})\delta_{v=\bar{v}}(v)
as
t\to\infty
.
